*Prepared 2026-10-16 for an independent, text-only adversarial reviewer. Send this whole file. **It is self-contained**: no earlier packet, review, note or brief is needed or incorporated. Sections 0–12 are under review. Section 13 is the reviewer's instructions.*

**Supersession.** Revision 21 supersedes every earlier packet in full for review purposes. Only text in this file is in force.

No code, script, hook, setting, memory record or operational state was modified. No fixture, test, git command or operational experiment was run to produce this packet. Nothing was implemented, committed or pushed. G-F is unchanged (CLOSED). A2.6 is not authorised. UR-19, UR-20, UR-21, the new **UR-22**, P-06, P-19, P-21, P-25 and every other deferred item stay with the owner. SPSM was not accessed. The interim fail-closed behaviour ((a) × B) is unchanged; R21 makes it stricter in one place (UR-22 interim) and corrects one over-strict rationale (R20-01).

**What changed, in one paragraph.** The Revision 20 review found the relevance rule (entry(End,p) ≠ entry(Pre,p)) sound in every mode and no false MERGED; its findings are about scope and wording. Revision 21: (R20-01) restricts the K22 non-textual-current-target rule to C-text paths with a **surviving touched line** (survival class S(i)), so relevance is no longer mistaken for survival, and states the complete set of results over-selection can add (K25, K30, K31, K32, K34 and failure rows — never MERGED); (R20-04) analyses the survival-key asymmetry in paired histories, finds the symmetric key (A) justified by the key's own stated rationale but neither A nor the current key (B) uniformly stricter, puts the choice to the owner as **UR-22**, and adopts as interim the **strict combination** (at least as strict as both); (R20-02) ends the repeated-calls sequence at FINALIZE's last call so snap1 is always issued afresh; (R20-03) corrects stale owner-facing text; (R20-05) narrows CV1's parenthetical; (R20-06) replaces the circular canonical-order trigger with one decided from the compiled options. ## 0. Status, labels, conventions

**Status.** This is proposed design text, not approved. A review PASS does not authorise implementation. Implementation and commit each need their own authorisation.

**Evidence labels**

| Label | Meaning |
|---|---|
| [M] | Measured earlier; reported only (§7.6). Not re-measured for this revision. |
| [R] | Reasoned |
| [P] | Proposed fixture; **unexecuted** (all fixtures in this packet) |
| [U] | Unresolved; fails closed |

**Class abbreviations**

| Abbreviation | Meaning |
|---|---|
| POS / NEG / INSUF / CONTRA / NONE | Freshness evidence classes: positive, negative, insufficient, contradictory, absent |
| TF | `outcome=TOOL_FAILURE` |
| AMB | `outcome=AMBIGUOUS` |
| PDB | **Pre-decision behaviour.** The normative behaviour that applies to a PROPOSED item until the owner decides. |
| Interim | For an owner question (UR-n), the fail-closed behaviour that applies until the owner answers. Stated with each question in §2.3. |

**Fixture status (R12Δ, R11-08)**

| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **B** | BINDING. **Every** asserted field is independent of every open register item. Only failure-line (TF) fixtures qualify in this revision: every classified line carries `origin_identity`, which depends on P-25(i) (§2.2). |
| **Bo** | BINDING OUTCOME. `outcome`, `trace`, `recommend`, `caveats` and every integration and content field are binding. **`origin_identity` and the freshness fields** (`fresh`, `observed_at`, `freshness_source`, `freshness_trace`, `freshness_reason`, `fetch_head`) are stated as interim values and follow the P-25(i) field mapping (§2.2). Used only where that mapping changes none of the binding fields. |
| **B(outcome); X O(P-n)** | `outcome`, `trace`, `fresh`, `observed_at` and `recommend` are binding. Field or text X depends on P-n. `origin_identity` follows §2.2 as for Bo. |
| **O(P-n)** | Pending owner decision on a proposal. The fixture gives the interim (PDB) expectation and the if-adopted / if-rejected expectation where it differs. |
| **O(P-25i)** | A special case of O. The fixture's outcome fields change under P-25 sub-decision (i), following §2.2. |
| **O(UR-n)** (R12Δ) | Pending an owner question (UR-19 or UR-21). The fixture gives the interim (fail-closed) expectation and each option's expectation where it differs. |
| **V(UR-n)** | Pending verification. |
| **GATED** | Unreachable while G-F is CLOSED. |
| **OUT-OF-MODEL** | Demonstrates a limitation. Never a pass. |

"Binding-outcome fixture" in the coverage count (§3) means a fixture with status B, Bo or B(outcome).

## 1. Normative specification

### 1.1 Scope and terms

**Scope [B-01, B-02].** A read-only helper plus isolated adversarial fixtures that replace A2.5 Observe step 4.
- The helper emits one line, including `caveats`.
- Observe prints the line, the §1.10 wording for `recommend`, then the fixed text of each caveat token in order.
- Observe derives nothing itself.

**Non-goals**
- D2–D5 and A2.6.
- Deployment, production or completion status.
- MR or author attribution.
- Writing to any repository; changing memory, status or NEXT.
- Semantic revert detection.
- Local `.git` forgery.
- Canonical-upstream identity (P-25, L-15).
- Effective transport of a past fetch (L-16).
- Line provenance (L-4).

**Terms**

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **T** | Object id of `refs/remotes/origin/development`, resolved once. Never local `development`. |
| **X ⊑ Y** | `merge-base --is-ancestor X Y`: 0 → true, 1 → false, any other exit → C10. X ⋢ Y is its negation. |
| **present(X)** | X ⊑ T |
| **np(X)** | Parent count, from a strict `cat-file commit X` parse (≤ 64 KiB) |
| **parent0(X)** | X^1, or the empty tree `4b825dc642cb6eb9a060e54bf8d69288fbee4904` if np(X) = 0 |
| **paths(X)** | `diff-tree -r --no-renames --name-only -z parent0(X) X`, NUL-split and sorted |
| **paths(A,B)** | `diff-tree -r --no-renames --name-only -z A B`, NUL-split and sorted. paths(IU) = paths(I.base, I.tip). |
| **FP** | `rev-list --first-parent T`, newest first; pos(X) counts from the oldest end |
| **P** | When present(C): the oldest X ∈ FP with C ⊑ X |
| **GitLab pair (S, M)** | M ∈ FP, np(M) = 2, S = M^2, np(S) = 1, S^1 = M^1, tree(S) = tree(M) |
| **entry(X, p)** | (mode, type, oid) from `ls-tree -z --full-tree X -- p`, or ⊥. Types: blob (100644/100755), symlink (blob 120000), gitlink (commit 160000), tree (040000). Equality compares all three components. ⊥ equals only ⊥. **entry(empty tree, p) = ⊥ for every p, without a git call** (R12Δ, R11-09). |
| **I** | (via, base, tip, end, integrating, merge, seg_base, lineage_root, landing) |
| **CU** | (parent0(C), C) |
| **IU** | (I.base, I.tip) |

### 1.2 Inputs

**Argument model**
- argv[0] is the program name and never a positional.
- `--` ends option parsing.
- A value-taking option consumes the next element verbatim, even if it begins with `-`.
- Any other element beginning with `-` is an unknown option (A08). Everything else is positional.
- Options:
  - `--repo <path>`
  - `--now <TS>`
  - `--trace-argv <path>`
  - `--expect-origin <url>` [P-25 · PDB: not accepted → A08; if adopted: accepted, see R10 row]

**Phase A rows** (first match wins, in this order):

| Order | Row | Condition | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | A08 | Unknown option; value-taking option as last element; `--opt=value`; `--now` or `--trace-argv` repeated | bad-input |
| 2 | A06 | `--repo` absent or repeated | bad-input |
| 3 | A07 | Positional count ≠ 1 | bad-input |
| 4 | A01 | C fails `^[0-9a-f]{40}$` | bad-input |
| 5 | A05 | `--now` fails TS syntax, is not a calendar instant, or is outside 1970-01-01T00:00:00Z … 9999-12-31T23:59:59Z | bad-input |
| 6 | A02 | `--repo` not absolute, has a `.`/`..` component, or ≠ its realpath | bad-input |
| 7 | A03 | `<repo>` or `<repo>/.git` not a real directory | integrity-refused |
| 8 | A04 | `st_uid(.git)` ≠ euid | unsafe-ownership |

**Clocks**
1. **Monotonic time** is used only for C09 (60 s per call) and C08 (300 s total).
2. **Wall-clock `now`** is integer seconds UTC. Without `--now`, it is floor(time.time()), sampled **once, immediately after snap0a**. With `--now`, it is the given value, and the system clock is not read for `now`.
3. **Stored timestamps** (reflog ts, FETCH_HEAD mtime) come from other processes' clocks.

**Comparisons**
- Integer seconds throughout.
- FETCH_HEAD is future iff floor(mtime_ns/10⁹) > now.
- 24 h means 0 ≤ now − observed_at ≤ 86400.
- A stored time later than `now` gives CONTRA `future`. **This is not a claim that a race occurred.**
- A write after snap0a produces D02.
- Missing data: R1/F01/F09. Malformed: R2/F02/F07. Inconsistent: R3.

**Test-only inputs:** `--now`, `--trace-argv`, and a library-only harness interface ([harness]) with phase hooks, git-result substitution and `time.time` injection.

### 1.3 Controlled environment

Every git call is an argv array, run with no shell, from a private empty temporary working directory:

```
env -i PATH=/usr/bin:/bin HOME=<helper-private empty dir> LC_ALL=C TZ=UTC
       GIT_DIR=<repo>/.git GIT_WORK_TREE=<repo> GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES=<parent of repo>
       GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1 GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null GIT_ATTR_NOSYSTEM=1
       GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0 GIT_NO_REPLACE_OBJECTS=1 GIT_LITERAL_PATHSPECS=1 GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0
  /usr/bin/git --no-replace-objects --no-pager --literal-pathspecs
    -c core.fsmonitor=false -c core.commitGraph=false -c core.attributesFile=/dev/null
    -c core.quotePath=true -c core.bigFileThreshold=512m
    -c diff.context=3 -c diff.interHunkContext=0 -c diff.indentHeuristic=true
    -c diff.suppressBlankEmpty=false -c diff.relative=false -c diff.noprefix=false
    -c diff.mnemonicPrefix=false -c diff.renames=false -c diff.algorithm=myers
    -c diff.submodule=short -c diff.ignoreSubmodules=none
    -c log.showSignature=false -c gpg.program=/bin/false -c gpg.ssh.program=/bin/false
    -c gpg.x509.program=/bin/false -c patchid.verbatim=true -c patchid.stable=<UR-4>
```

**Allowlist**

| Command | Allowed forms |
|---|---|
| `version` | — |
| `rev-parse` | `--absolute-git-dir`, `--is-bare-repository`, `--is-shallow-repository`, `--show-object-format`, `--verify -q <oid\|ref\|oid:path>` |
| `symbolic-ref -q` | — |
| `cat-file` | `-t`, `-s`, `-e`, `blob <oid>`, `commit <oid>` |
| `ls-tree` | `-z --full-tree <commit> -- <path>` |
| `rev-list` | bounded forms in §1.8 and §6 |
| `merge-base` | `--all`, `--is-ancestor` |
| `for-each-ref` | `--contains`, `--format` |
| `diff` | DIFF form only |
| `diff-tree` | `-r --no-renames --name-only -z`; `--cc --name-only -z --no-ext-diff --no-textconv` |
| `patch-id` | `--verbatim` |
| `config` | `--list --show-scope -z` |

**Forbidden:** everything else, including `log`, `show`, `reflog`, `fetch`, `pull`, `ls-remote`, `gc`, `fsck`, `merge-tree`, `--textconv`, `--filters`.

**Exit codes.** Any non-zero exit → C10, except these documented false results:

| Call | Exit | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `--is-ancestor` | 1 | false |
| `rev-parse --verify -q` | 1 | absent |
| `symbolic-ref -q` | 1 | not symbolic |
| `cat-file -e` | 1 | absent |

**Limits.** stdout > 64 MiB → C08. A call longer than 60 s → C09. stderr is discarded. Paths come only from `-z` output and are passed as literal pathspecs.

**No side effects [R].** No hooks, no pager, `--no-ext-diff --no-textconv`, the C05 refusals, gpg disabled, no index or worktree.

### 1.4 Preflight

**Configuration.**
- Read once with `config --list --show-scope -z`. Records are `scope\0key\nvalue\0`, split at the first `\n`. git lowercases section and key names; subsections keep their case.
- Only the `local` and `command` scopes are visible (because of `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM` and `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null`). `command` is the helper's own pins and is excluded from checks.
- The `worktree` scope is refused (C04), and `include` is refused (C05).
- Global and system configuration is never read (L-16). The helper never parses config bytes itself, and the snapshot guards `.git/config`.

**Phase C rows**

| Row | Condition | Result |
|---|---|---|
| C01 | `git version` does not match `^git version 2\.43\.[0-9]+$` | unsupported-version |
| C02 | `rev-parse --absolute-git-dir` exits non-zero (this includes git's own refusal of a repository owned by another user), or its output ≠ realpath(`<repo>/.git`), or `rev-parse --is-bare-repository` prints `true`, or `--is-shallow-repository` prints `true` | preflight |
| C03 | object format ≠ sha1 | object-format |
| C04 | Integrity refusal: any of `shallow`, `info/grafts`, `objects/info/alternates`, `objects/info/http-alternates`, `refs/replace/*`, `config.worktree`, `commondir` present or non-empty; non-empty `info/attributes`; `extensions.*` other than `objectFormat=sha1` / `refStorage=files` | integrity-refused |
| C05 | Configuration refusal: `include.*`, `includeIf.*`, `diff.*.command/textconv/binary/funcname/xfuncname/wordRegex/cachetextconv`, `diff.external`, `diff.orderFile`, `core.attributesFile`, `core.alternateRefsCommand`, `core.sshCommand`, `core.worktree`, `gpg.*`, `log.showSignature`, `uploadpack.*`, `protocol.*` | config-refused |
| C06 | `refs/remotes/origin/development` absent | target-missing |
| C07 | Target symbolic, or loose ref file begins `ref:` | symbolic-target |

The redirection keys of OU1 (§1.6.3) are not refused here; they make OU fail. The configuration denylist cannot cover keys added in future git versions; the 2.43.x version pin bounds that risk.

### 1.5 Snapshot model

**Snapshot names**
- **snap0a** = the first `snap_files()` call, giving S0f.
- **snap0b** = `snap_refs()` inside phase C, giving S0r.
- **snap1** = the final `snap_files()` + `snap_refs()`, giving S1f and S1r.

**File records:** (relpath, kind ∈ {absent, regular, dir}, st_dev, st_ino, size, mtime_ns, ctime_ns, sha256) [P-17 · PDB: include ctime/inode; if rejected: exclude them].
- Opened with `O_NOFOLLOW`, inside realpath(`.git`), ≤ 4 MiB.
- Non-regular or unreadable entries → read failure.
- Scope: `config`, `packed-refs`, `FETCH_HEAD`, the C04 files; recursively `refs/heads/`, `refs/remotes/origin/`, `refs/replace/` and `logs/refs/remotes/origin/`.

**Ref record:** S0r = (T, RAW, RH).
- RAW = output of `for-each-ref --format='%(objectname) %(refname)' refs/heads/ refs/remotes/origin/`.
- RH = SHA-256(RAW).
- Equality compares (T, RH).

**`snapshot=` values (comparison scope)**

| Value | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `full` | File and ref records compared |
| `files-only` | S0r was never taken; only file records compared |
| `none` | Phase A or B01 |
| `final-unreadable` | D01 |

**Invariants**
- **I-1:** every classified line is `full`.
- **I-2:** `files-only` lets only a TF stand.
- **I-3:** every non-`full` line carries CV4.

phaseC returns `(prov, S0r)` and never raises.

**Snapshot value per row**

| Rows | Value |
|---|---|
| C01–C07; C10 from preflight / resolve_target / snap_refs | `files-only` |
| C10 from later calls | `full` |
| C08, C09, C13, C14 | by phase |
| C11, C12, C15 | `full` |

**What it detects [R]:** torn reads; change-then-restore via ctime [UR-12].

**Residuals:** object store; outside scope; coarse ticks; clock manipulation. No atomicity is claimed.

### 1.6 Freshness model

#### 1.6.1 Inputs

- snap0a bytes and stats; S0r.RAW; local configuration; `now`.
- TR = `logs/refs/remotes/origin/development`.
- OR = every file under `logs/refs/remotes/origin/`.
- FETCH_HEAD and its mtime.

#### 1.6.2 Reflog grammar

```
^([0-9a-f]{40}) ([0-9a-f]{40}) [^<\n]* <[^>\n]*> ([0-9]+) ([+-][0-9]{4})\t([^\n]*)$
```

- cmdline = the message before the first `": "`, or the whole message.
- The last line of a file is its newest entry.

#### 1.6.3 R rows

**OMS:** `remote.origin.fetch` is exactly one value, `+refs/heads/*:refs/remotes/origin/*`, and no other remote's fetch destination lies under `refs/remotes/origin/`.

**RA**

```
RA         = fetch-form / pull-form
fetch-form = "fetch" *(SP fopt) [SP "origin" *(SP (fopt / src))]
pull-form  = "pull"  *(SP popt) [SP "origin" *(SP (popt / src))]
fopt       = "--prune" / "-p"
popt       = "--tags" / "--rebase" / "--no-rebase" / "-r" / "--ff-only"
src        = 1*(ALPHA / DIGIT / "." / "_" / "/" / "-")   ; must not begin with "-" or "."
```

- A bare form (no `origin`) is accepted only if origin is the only remote.
- **Rejected:**
  - any other remote, `.`, or a URL;
  - a `src` containing `:`, `+`, `*`, `^` or `~`;
  - any option not listed above;
  - `--all` / `--multiple` [P-12 · PDB: rejected; if adopted: allowed with a single remote];
  - `update by push` [P-20 · PDB: rejected].
- A command line proves nothing about the remote.

**OU — origin URL policy (renamed steps; SP0 moved first; IPv6 by value; totality fixes)**

The steps run in order, and the first decisive step sets `origin_url_class`.

| Step | Condition | Result |
|---|---|---|
| **OU0** | `remote.origin.url` absent, or more than one value | FAIL `absent` |
| **OU1** | Local-scope key present (lowercased): `url.*.insteadof`, `url.*.pushinsteadof`, `remote.origin.uploadpack`, `remote.origin.proxy`, `remote.origin.vcs`, `http.proxy`, `http.*.proxy`, `http.curloptresolve`, `http.*.curloptresolve`, `core.gitproxy` | FAIL `rewritten` (key existence and semantics: UR-18) |
| **OU2** | u contains `\` | FAIL `malformed` |
| **OU3** | u matches `^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9+.-]*::` | FAIL `transport-helper` |
| **OU4** | u matches `^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9+.-]*://` | → SC1 |
| **OU5** | u matches `^(?:[^@/:\[\]]+@)?(\[[^\]]+\]\|[^/:\[\]]+):(.+)$` and the first `:` comes before any `/` | → SP0 |
| **OU6** | otherwise | FAIL `local-path` |

**Scheme URL**

| Step | Rule | On failure |
|---|---|---|
| **SC1** | Lowercase the scheme; it must be one of {https, http, ssh, git} | FAIL `scheme` |
| **SC2** | Authority = text after `://` up to the first `/`, `?` or `#`, or end of string. Must be non-empty. | FAIL `malformed` |
| **SC3** | If the authority contains `@`, userinfo is everything up to the **last** `@`. Userinfo containing `[` or `]` is invalid. Remove the userinfo. | FAIL `malformed` |
| **SC4** | Split host and port: see below | FAIL `malformed` |

**SC4 detail**
- If the remainder starts with `[`: the literal runs to the first `]`; what follows must be empty or `:`PORT; the literal must contain `:`. Then go to HS4.
- Otherwise, if the remainder contains `:`: the part after the last `:` must be a PORT, and no other `:` may appear. The host is what precedes it.
- **PORT = 1–5 digits whose value is 1–65535, applied identically in both branches.** An empty host or invalid PORT → FAIL `malformed`.
- Non-bracketed host → HS1.

**scp form**

| Step | Rule | Result |
|---|---|---|
| **SP0** (R10-04, evaluated first) | Host (group 1) unbracketed and exactly one character | FAIL `local-path` |
| **SP1** | Path (group 2) must be non-empty | else FAIL `malformed` |
| **SP2** | Bracketed host whose content contains `:`: if the content parses as IPv6 (HS4 rules) → HS4; else it must match `^[^:]+:PORT$`, and the host part → HS1; else FAIL `malformed`. Bracketed host without `:`: strip the brackets → HS1. Unbracketed host → HS1. | — |

**Host checks**

| Step | Rule | Result |
|---|---|---|
| **HS1** | Lowercase, then strip one trailing `.`. If every dot-separated label matches `^(0x[0-9a-f]*\|[0-9]+)$` → HS3. Otherwise the host must match `^[a-z0-9]([a-z0-9-]*[a-z0-9])?(\.[a-z0-9]([a-z0-9-]*[a-z0-9])?)*$` and be ≤ 253 characters → HS5. | else FAIL `malformed` |
| **HS3** | more than 4 labels → FAIL `malformed`. 1–4 labels follow inet_aton rules: `0x` prefix = hex (`0x` alone → malformed); leading `0` on a multi-digit label = octal (digits 8/9 → malformed); otherwise decimal. The last label fills the remaining bytes (32 / 24 / 16 / 8 bits for 1 / 2 / 3 / 4 labels). Overflow → malformed. | Value in 127.0.0.0/8 or 0.0.0.0/8 → FAIL `local-host`; otherwise **pass** |
| **HS4** | parse to a **128-bit value** (RFC 4291, `::` compression, embedded dotted IPv4). Zone ids (`%`) or parse failure → FAIL `malformed`. Reject by **value** (see below). | rejected → FAIL `local-host`; otherwise **pass** |
| **HS5** | `localhost`, or a name ending `.localhost` | FAIL `local-host`; otherwise **pass** (`network-form`) |

HS4 rejects these values:
- `::1` (loopback);
- `::` (unspecified);
- `::ffff:0:0/96` (IPv4-mapped) whose low 32 bits lie in 127.0.0.0/8 or 0.0.0.0/8;
- `::/96` (IPv4-compatible) whose low 32 bits lie in 127.0.0.0/8 or 0.0.0.0/8 (`::` and `::1` are already covered above).

Spelling does not matter: `[0:0:0:0:0:0:0:1]`, `[0::1]`, `[::ffff:7f00:1]` and `[::7f00:1]` are all local-host.

**Determinism [R].** The steps are ordered, every step either decides or names exactly one next step, and every predicate is total. Every u therefore reaches exactly one class: network-form, absent, rewritten, malformed, transport-helper, scheme, local-path or local-host.

**What OU establishes, and what it does not.** Passing OU means only that the **repository-local** configuration names origin with a network-form URL, the host is not a recognised loopback, unspecified or `.localhost` value, and no OU1 key is present locally. It does **not** establish any of the following (L-16):
- that the host is public, reachable, trustworthy or canonical;
- name resolution;
- global or system configuration;
- environment variables at the time of the fetch;
- SSH host aliases;
- that the URL has not changed since the fetch.

**R rows** (first match wins)

| Order | Row | Condition | Class |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | R1 | TR absent or empty | NONE |
| 2 | R2 | malformed line | CONTRA(malformed) |
| 3 | R3 | an earlier ts > the last ts | CONTRA(non-monotonic) |
| 4 | R4 | last new ≠ T | CONTRA(newest-not-T) |
| 5 | R5 | last ts > now | CONTRA(future) |
| 6 | R6 | last cmdline ∉ RA | INSUF(newest-unaccepted) |
| 7 | R8 | OMS fails | INSUF(nonstandard-origin-mapping) |
| 8 | R9 | OU fails | INSUF(nonstandard-origin-url) |
| 9 | R10 | [P-25] pin given and norm(pin) ≠ norm(u) → origin-pin-mismatch; **or** P-25 adopted as mandatory and no pin given → origin-pin-missing | INSUF |
| 10 | R7 | otherwise | POS(ts) |

**`origin_identity`**

| Value | When |
|---|---|
| `pinned` | A pin was given and it matches |
| `pin-mismatch` | A pin was given and it differs |
| `pin-missing` | P-25 adopted as mandatory, no pin given |
| `unpinned` | Every other case. Always `unpinned` under the PDB. |

#### 1.6.4 F rows

**Definitions**

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| norm(u) | Strip userinfo, then one trailing `/`, then one trailing `.git`. Byte-exact, case-sensitive. |
| origin lines | FETCH_HEAD lines with norm(url) = norm(remote.origin.url) |
| D | Origin lines `branch 'development'` |
| D\* | All `branch 'development'` lines |
| WF [P-08] | RA fetch forms with no `src` (bare only with a single remote; `--all` [P-12]) |
| LAST | Last lines of the non-empty OR files |
| ts\* | max ts over LAST |
| Enew | Lines of LAST with ts = ts\* (undefined if LAST = ∅) |

FETCH_HEAD line grammar:

```
^([0-9a-f]{40})\t(not-for-merge)?\t(branch|tag|remote-tracking branch) '([^'\n]*)' of ([^\n]+)$
```

**Evaluation** (a stop is terminal for F; fc is the first failed corroboration check)

| Step | Row | Check | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | F01 | FETCH_HEAD absent or empty | NONE; stop |
| 2 | F02 | grammar failure | CONTRA; stop |
| 3 | F03 | floor(mtime_ns/10⁹) > now | CONTRA; stop |
| 4 | F04 / F05 | insteadOf or pushInsteadOf present / remote.origin.url not single-valued | → 4a |
| 4a | F23 | some D\* line has sha ≠ T [P-23 · PDB: NEG; if rejected → 4b] | NEG; stop |
| 4b | F04 / F05 | — | INSUF; stop |
| 5 | F24 | only if P-08 is rejected: some D ≠ T and P-01 in force → FROW F21 (NEG); otherwise FROW F24 (INSUF `corroboration-not-applied`) | stop |
| 6 | F06 | not OMS [P-08] | fc |
| 7 | F07 | an OR file malformed [P-08] | fc; skip 8–17 |
| 8 | F09 | LAST = ∅ [P-08] | fc; skip 9–17 |
| 9 | F08 | ts\* > now [P-08] | CONTRA; stop |
| 10 | F10 | Enew cmdlines differ [P-08] | fc |
| 11 | F12 | `--all` form ∧ P-12 adopted ∧ > 1 remote [P-08, P-12] | fc |
| 12 | F11 | Enew cmdline ∉ WF [P-08] | fc |
| 13 | F13 | a non-origin line present [P-08] | fc |
| 14 | F14 | duplicate `branch '<n>'` origin lines [P-08] | fc |
| 15 | F15 | content link: each e ∈ Enew with new ≠ 0{40} must have exactly one origin line `branch '<name>'` with sha = e.new [P-08] | fc |
| 16 | F16 | completeness: excluding development and HEAD, origin branch lines must be in bijection with the tracking refs in S0r.RAW, with equal values [P-08] | fc |
| 17 | F17 | \|floor(mtime_ns/10⁹) − ts\*\| > W [P-08; P-11 · PDB two-sided; if rejected: one-sided (fails only if mtime_s < ts\* − W); UR-1 · W = 120 s] | fc |

**Development decision** (evaluated in this order)

| Row | Condition | Result |
|---|---|---|
| F19 | corroborated ∧ D ≠ T | NEG `negative-conflict` [B-12] |
| F21 | ¬corroborated ∧ D ≠ T | [P-01 · PDB: NEG `negative-conflict-uncorroborated`; if rejected: INSUF, fc row and token] |
| F20 | corroborated ∧ \|D\| = 0 | [P-10 · PDB: NEG `negative-dev-absent`; if rejected: INSUF `dev-absent-ignored`] |
| F22 | corroborated ∧ \|D\| = 1 ∧ D = T ∧ G-F CLOSED | INSUF `unverified-association` |
| F18 | as F22, with G-F OPEN | POS `ok` — **unreachable** |
| — | otherwise | INSUF, fc row and token |

**fc → fetch_head**

| fc | fetch_head |
|---|---|
| F06 | `uncorroborated-refspec` |
| F07 | `uncorroborated-reflog-malformed` |
| F09 | `uncorroborated-no-fetch-event` |
| F10 | `uncorroborated-multiple-fetch-events` |
| F12 | `uncorroborated-multi-remote` |
| F11 | `uncorroborated-not-whole-fetch` |
| F13 | `uncorroborated-foreign-lines` |
| F14 | `uncorroborated-duplicate-lines` |
| F15 | `uncorroborated-no-link` |
| F16 | `uncorroborated-incomplete` |
| F17 | `uncorroborated-no-reflog` if ts\* < mtime_s − W, else `uncorroborated-window` |

**Terminal rows**

| Row | fetch_head |
|---|---|
| F01 | `none` |
| F02 | `malformed` |
| F03 / F08 | `future` |
| F04 | `inadmissible-insteadof` |
| F05 | `inadmissible-remote-url` |
| F23 | `negative-conflict-unattributed` |
| F24 | `corroboration-not-applied` |

**FROW.** The terminal row if F stopped (F24 per step 5); else the decision row; else the fc row. Exactly one FROW per input.

**mtime** is used only in F03 and F17, and only as the file's write time [B-11].

#### 1.6.5 Combination (every field stated)

| Row | R | F | observed_at | fresh | source | reason |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Cb1 | any | NEG | none | no | none | blocking order |
| Cb2 | CONTRA | not NEG | none | no | none | blocking order |
| Cb3 | not CONTRA | CONTRA | none | no | none | blocking order |
| Cb4 | POS(t_R) | POS(t\*) | max | yes iff 0 ≤ now − obs ≤ 86400 | both | ok/stale — GATED |
| Cb5 | POS(t_R) | INSUF/NONE | t_R | yes iff 0 ≤ now − t_R ≤ 86400 | reflog | ok/stale |
| Cb6 | INSUF(R6)/NONE | POS(t\*) | t\* | yes iff ≤ 86400 | fetch_head | ok/stale — GATED [P-09] |
| Cb7 | INSUF(R6/R8/R9/R10) | INSUF/NONE | none | no | none | newest-unaccepted / nonstandard-origin-mapping / nonstandard-origin-url / origin-pin-mismatch / origin-pin-missing (per the R row) |
| Cb8 | NONE | INSUF/NONE | none | no | none | no-evidence |
| Cb9 | INSUF(R8/R9/R10) | POS | none | no | none | as Cb7 — GATED |

- Every (R class, F class) pair has exactly one row.
- **Blocking order:** conflict > conflict-unattributed > dev-absent > conflict-uncorroborated > future > newest-not-T > non-monotonic > malformed > fetch-head-malformed.
- **While G-F is CLOSED, the only positive path is R7 → Cb5.**

#### 1.6.6 Gate G-F

**CLOSED.** Opening it requires all of:
- UR-7, UR-8, UR-9 and UR-10 verified;
- UR-1 measured;
- owner approval of P-08, P-11, P-12 and P-09.

While CLOSED:
- F18, Cb4, Cb6 and Cb9 are unreachable;
- no FETCH_HEAD evidence can make fresh=yes;
- F19, F20, F21 and F23 are still evaluated.

#### 1.6.7 Unchanged-target fetch [B-13]

| Case | Description | Freshness |
|---|---|---|
| UF1 | Other refs moved | never fresh (F22) |
| UF2 | Nothing moved | never fresh — **UR-2, owner question** |
| UF3 | Partial fetch or no-op pull | never fresh |

**UR-2 options:**
- (a) admit FETCH_HEAD mtime as a file-level time;
- (b) `ls-remote`;
- (c) a fetch recorder;
- (d) accept the limitation. **Option (d) applies meanwhile.**


### 1.7 Patch identity, anchoring, landing and content — R21Δ (R20-01..R20-06); R20Δ (R19-01..R19-06); R19Δ (R18-01, -02, -03, -04, -07, -08); R18Δ (R17-01, -02, -04, -06); R17Δ (R16-01, -02, -03, -05, -06); R16Δ (R15-02, -03, -06, -07); R15Δ (R14-01, -02, -04, -05); R14Δ (R13-01, R13-02); R13Δ (R12-01, -05, -06, -08); R12Δ (R11-01, -02, -04, -05, -09, -10, -11, -13)

#### 1.7.1 Claims

| Evidence | What it shows |
|---|---|
| Patch-id equality | Net-diff equivalence under the pinned diff (what equality implies is UR-16). It **identifies the integration** (IA3–IA5). It never establishes landing by itself. |
| Entry equality | Identical (mode, type, oid) |
| Line counts | Net per-file counts with **no provenance**. Identical text lines cannot be told apart, so no claim is ever made about *which* copy of a line is C's. |
| **Landing** (R12Δ) | For every relevant path, the integrated state Tip agrees with C's branch end End at that path. **In force (interim, UR-21 open): by state equality only**, entry(Tip,p) = entry(End,p), in every mode. **Only if the owner adopts UR-21 Option A:** **landing-textual** squash paths may also be landed by **count-level** agreement (the target-count guard plus the carried-by-unit check, §1.7.3). That evidence is count-level: it is not provenance, and it does not show that an equal count came from C rather than from an independent equivalent change. |

#### 1.7.2 DIFF and pid

```
DIFF(A,B) := git --attr-source=4b825dc642cb6eb9a060e54bf8d69288fbee4904 diff -U3 --no-relative
             --no-ext-diff --no-textconv --no-color --binary --full-index --diff-algorithm=myers
             --no-renames --ignore-submodules=none --submodule=short --src-prefix=a/ --dst-prefix=b/
             -O/dev/null <A> <B>
pid(A,B)  := DIFF(A,B) | git patch-id --verbatim
```

0 lines → NULL. Exactly one line matching `^[0-9a-f]{40} [0-9a-f]{40}$` → its first field. Anything else → C11. A and B may be commits or the empty tree.

#### 1.7.3 Anchoring and landing (R12Δ)

**States**

| Name | Definition |
|---|---|
| Pre | parent0(C) (the empty tree if np(C) = 0) |
| Post | C |
| End | I.end |
| Tb | I.base (the target before integration) |
| Tip | I.tip |
| Tn | T |
| Bu | Branch-side unit base: Pre (direct); seg_base (squash: B_X for a first-integration match, X_v for a chained match); the single member of `merge-base --all P^1 P^2` (ancestry-merge; 0 → K28, > 1 → K29) |
| **Ht** (R12Δ, R11-04) | **Absorbed-target base** — the newest target state the unit base has absorbed. Ht = Bu for ancestry-merge and first-integration squash (Bu ⊑ T by construction: it is a merge base with the target side). For a **chained** squash (Bu = X_v, which is not in T), Ht is the single member of `merge-base --all X_v T`; 0 or > 1 members → **K34**. For ancestry-direct, Ht = Pre. |

**State kind (R13Δ, R12-01, R12-06).** For a state X and a path p, kind(X,p) is exactly one of:
- **absent** — entry(X,p) = ⊥;
- **tree** (040000), **gitlink** (160000), **symlink** (120000);
- **binary** — a regular blob (100644/100755) with a NUL in its first 8000 bytes;
- **text** — any other regular blob, **including the empty blob** and a blob whose lines are all whitespace.

A state is **textual** at p iff its kind is text or absent. **blob(X,p)** := the oid component of entry(X,p) if kind(X,p) ∈ {text, binary}, else ⊥. Comparisons of blob(·) and entry(·) are therefore always defined (⊥ equals only ⊥). X(L), the count of line L at p, is defined **only for a textual state** (absent counts as 0) and is never computed on a non-textual state.

**content and mode (R18Δ, R17-01).** content(X,p) := (kind(X,p), oid of entry(X,p)) when kind(X,p) ∈ {text, binary, symlink, gitlink}; := (tree) when kind(X,p) = tree (the directory's oid is ignored: the files inside it are separate paths of C and are judged on their own); := ⊥ when absent. mode(X,p) := the mode component of entry(X,p), or ⊥. entry(X,p) = entry(Y,p) iff mode, type and oid all agree; content and mode separate the "what" from the "permissions".

**Survival key (R19Δ; R21Δ, R20-04: kind-changing paths made explicit).** For a C-opaque path p whose content C changed (content(Post,p) ≠ content(Pre,p)):
- p is **kind-changing** iff kind(Pre,p) ≠ kind(Post,p) (absent, text, binary, symlink, gitlink and tree are distinct kinds);
- **content survives** iff content(End,p) = content(Post,p) (for a tree result, content = (tree), so this equals kind survival);
- **kind survives** iff kind(End,p) = kind(Post,p).

How survival is used depends on **UR-22** (owner question, §2.3):
- **Interim (strict, fails closed):** p is a member of **B** (its current-target state is checked by b) iff its content survives, **or** it is kind-changing and its kind survives. p counts toward **superseded** iff its content does not survive (so a kind-surviving, content-replaced path counts in both). For **content state 2 (K21)** only members of B whose content survives count (B\*), so a C whose only change is a kind change later edited on the branch is still K21.
- **Option A (symmetric kind key):** for every kind-changing p, survival is judged by kind; for others by content. B = surviving paths; superseded = non-surviving; state 2 uses B.
- **Option B (Revision 19–20 asymmetric key):** survival by kind only when kind(Post) = tree, or kind(Post) = text and kind(Pre) non-textual; otherwise by content. B = surviving; superseded = non-surviving; state 2 uses B.

**sk(X,p)** below denotes the key that decides "content survives" (content, or kind for a tree result); kind survival is used only as stated above.

**C-text and C-opaque paths (R13Δ).** A path p ∈ paths(C) is **C-text** iff Pre and Post are both textual at p; otherwise it is **C-opaque**. This depends on C alone.

**Touched lines.** For a C-text path p whose End state is textual, a line L is **touched** iff S_A(L) + S_R(L) > 0 (§1.7.4). Text comparison drops whitespace-only lines and compares byte-exact.

**Relevance and survival — separated (R20Δ, R19-01).** Two different questions are answered by two different rules.

**Relevant (subject to landing).** A path p ∈ paths(C) is **relevant** iff **entry(End,p) ≠ entry(Pre,p)** — the branch end still differs from C's parent at p, in content, kind or file mode (⊥ equals only ⊥). Only the entries of Pre and End are compared; Bu, Tb, Tip and Tn never affect it. This is a **scrutiny** rule, not a survival claim: it also selects a path whose branch-end state differs from Pre only because of later branch commits (C's change superseded, or deleted and then re-added differently). Selecting too much can only add AMBIGUOUS results — K25 (candidate anchoring), K30, K31 (landing), K32, K34 (anchoring) — and TOOL_FAILURE rows from the extra reads (C08, C09, C10); since R21 restricts the K22 current-target rule to S(i) paths, it no longer adds K22; it can never add a MERGED (R21Δ, R20-01; §11.77).

**Survival classes (which of C's changes survive to the branch end).** Used by the content checks (§1.7.4), CV1/CV3 wording, superseded and Option A eligibility — **never to exclude a path from landing**:
- **S(i)** p is C-text, End is textual at p, and p has a touched line;
- **S(ii)** p is C-opaque, content(Post,p) ≠ content(Pre,p), and p survives under the UR-22 rule in force (interim: content survives, or p is kind-changing and its kind survives);
- **S(iii)** a mode change: entry(Pre,p) ≠ ⊥, entry(Post,p) ≠ ⊥, mode(Post,p) ≠ mode(Pre,p), entry(End,p) ≠ ⊥ and mode(End,p) = mode(Post,p);
- **S(iv)** p is C-text, End is textual at p, blob(Pre,p) ≠ blob(Post,p), no line at p has A(L) + R(L) > 0, and blob(End,p) = blob(Post,p).

Earlier revisions called S(i)–S(iv) "rules (i)–(iv)" and used them as the relevance test; fixture text "relevant by (k)" now reads "relevant (End ≠ Pre), with survival class S(k)".

**Every path with a survival class is relevant [R].** S(i): a touched line means End(L) ≠ Pre(L) for some L, so the blobs differ. S(ii): sk(End) = sk(Post) ≠ sk(Pre) (a change of content keyed by content, or of kind keyed by kind) implies content(End) ≠ content(Pre). S(iii): mode(End) = mode(Post) ≠ mode(Pre). S(iv): blob(End) = blob(Post) ≠ blob(Pre). In each case entry(End,p) ≠ entry(Pre,p).

**Why no surviving change can drop out of relevance (R20Δ) [R].** If any part of C's change at p — a line, an object, a kind or a mode that C put there — is still present at the branch end, then the branch end differs from C's parent at p, so p is relevant. Contrapositive: a path that is not relevant has entry(End,p) = entry(Pre,p); the branch end is then exactly C's parent there, and **no** part of C's change exists at the branch end. The argument uses no kinds, counts or survival keys. It replaces the per-kind arguments of Revisions 13–19, each of which was falsified (R12-01, R17-01, R18-01, R19-01). A path that is not relevant takes no part in anchoring or landing; its counted and opaque changes are superseded (CV3), truthfully, because nothing of them is at the branch end.

**Landing-textual (R13Δ).** A relevant path is **landing-textual** iff Pre, Post, Bu, End, Tb and Tip are all textual at p. Only a landing-textual path may ever be landed by line counts (UR-21 Option A). Every other relevant path is landed only by state equality (r = e), or by construction in direct mode.

**Step 1 — Structural checks and anchoring (R14Δ, R13-02: separated)**

**Step 0 — structural checks (R16Δ, R15-02/R15-07: exactly these, nothing else).** These are not anchoring. They run in every mode and under every UR-19 and UR-21 option, and none reads Ht, Fk or AS:
- **before FINALIZE (§1.8.2):** the endpoint check (K27, K08), IA1–IA7 selection **except IA7 step 3** (candidate anchoring, below — R17Δ, R16-05), with K25 **for distinct X**, and the no-covering rows K10–K14;
- **in FINALIZE, ancestry-merge only:** Bu := `merge-base --all P^1 P^2` (0 → **K28**, > 1 → **K29**). Bu is needed by landing (ub), so it is structural.

**Step 0 failure continuation (R16Δ, R15-02).** If the Bu count gives K28 or K29, then **AS, Ht, Fk and landing are not computed**; AN := BU_NONE / BU_MULTI (that row) (R17Δ, R16-06), and nothing that reads Bu, ub, Ht or Fk runs. **Events, PR and content still run**, because none of them reads Bu (they read I.base = P^1, I.tip = P, I.end = P^2, Pre, Post and T). A C row they raise preempts K28/K29, as any C row preempts every K row. Otherwise K28/K29 is the decision. FX-K28h pins this.

**Candidate anchoring (R16Δ, R15-07; R17Δ: IA7 step 3).** When IA7 has several candidates for the same X, **IA7 step 3** computes AN for each candidate **before FINALIZE**. This is **anchoring**, not Step 0: it uses each candidate's AS, Ht and Fk exactly as below, runs only where AS ≠ ∅, and its K34/K32 results only feed IA7's K25 decision. A C row raised during it preempts every K row.

**Not structural and not anchoring.** The type guard and landing (Step 2) run **only if AN = ANCHORED**. Events (§6), PR and content (§1.7.4) are **always** computed in FINALIZE, whatever AN is (including K28/K29). A C row raised by any computation that runs preempts every K row; a skipped computation raises nothing. §1.8.3 and §4 state the same order.

**Read discipline and failure mapping (R16Δ; R17Δ, R16-03: staged AN and one canonical order).**
- **Every computation that runs reads its whole read set**, for every path in its domain, in the canonical order below; no value is read lazily or skipped because it turns out not to be needed. Read sets:
  - *relevance:* paths(C); entries and blobs of Pre, Post, End;
  - *AS* (only under (b) × Option A): the relevant paths; entries and blobs of Bu, Tb, Tip (kinds), and r, e;
  - *AN — in two stages (R17Δ):* **stage 1** — the decisions that need no entry: np(C) = 0 (no call); for a chained squash, `merge-base --all X_v T` (Ht); unless Ht = Pre, `merge-base --all Ht Pre` (Fk). If stage 1 yields K34, AN := FK_UNDET and **stage 2 is not run**: no entry of Ht, Fk or any member of a non-single Fk is read. **stage 2** — only when Ht and Fk are both single: the entries of Ht and Fk at the paths in AS. (Candidate anchoring in IA7 uses the same two stages per candidate.)
  - *LD:* the relevant paths; entries of Pre, Post, Bu, End, Tb, Tip; under Option A also their blobs (kinds and counts);
  - *EV:* §6.1 — u_I, u_C, r_I, r_C (each a DIFF, then `patch-id`), in that order; then the §6.2 records (pids of the §6.2 commits) (R18Δ);
  - *PR:* paths(C); entries of I.base, I.tip and T;
  - *CK:* paths(C); entries of Pre, Post, End, Tb, Tip, Tn, and the blobs of those six states for every C-text path (counts).
  - *DG (R18Δ):* entries of T and End at paths(C) (`drift`); `rev-list --count T --not I.tip -- paths(C)` (`later_commits`); `diff-tree -r --no-renames --name-only -z I.base I.tip` (`c_paths_in_unit`). Computed on **every** FINALIZE row; printed only where §1.11 says.
- **Canonical order (R18Δ, R17-04: one order, every call assigned).** One sequence, in this order:
  1. **Pre-FINALIZE, IA7 step 3 (candidate anchoring) — R21Δ, R20-06: triggered when IA7 step 2 has found several candidates for the same X and the compiled options allow a non-empty AS for a squash ((a) with either UR-21 option, or (b) with Option A; never (b′) or (b) × B):** relevance (computed once, then reused by FINALIZE — a computed value is never re-read); then, for each candidate in ascending **(OID(seg_base), OID(S))** (byte order of the hex ids): its AS; **its AN stage 1 and stage 2 only if that AS ≠ ∅**.
  2. **FINALIZE:** **Bu** (Step 0; ancestry-merge only) → **relevance** (unless already computed in 1) → **AS** → **AN** stage 1 → AN stage 2 → **LD** (only if AN = ANCHORED) → **EV** (§6.1: u_I, u_C, r_I, r_C, each a DIFF then patch-id, in that order; then §6.2 records in `rev-list` output order) → **PR** → **CK** → **DG** (diagnostics: `drift`, then `later_commits`, then `c_paths_in_unit`).
  3. **Always / skipped:** Bu, AS, AN, LD as stated above; **EV, PR, CK and DG always run** in FINALIZE (also after K28/K29/K32/K34), whatever is later printed; DG values are printed only where §1.11's presence rules say, but DG is computed on every FINALIZE row.
  - **Within a computation:** paths in **byte order** of their names; for each path, states in the fixed order **Pre, Post, Bu, Ht, Fk, End, Tb, Tip, Tn** (absent ones skipped); for each state, the entry (`ls-tree`) before the blob; **each blob read is `cat-file -s <oid>` immediately followed, if the size is at most 8 MiB, by `cat-file blob <oid>`** (R19Δ, R18-07; over 8 MiB → C08 at that position, the blob is not read).
  - **Repeated calls (R21Δ, R20-02: the sequence begins with the first git call after snap0a (preflight's `git version`) and ends with FINALIZE's last call (DG's `diff-tree` for `c_paths_in_unit`), in issue order — preflight, resolve_target, snap_refs, decide() including NB and paths(C), IA1–IA7, FINALIZE; **snap1's calls are outside the sequence, always issued afresh and never answered from it**, so the final ref record is a fresh read for D02):** a call whose argv was already issued earlier in the sequence is answered from that earlier result and not issued again; an **argv-keyed [harness] injection therefore fails at the first occurrence of that argv** in the sequence, and only there.
- **First failure decides (R17Δ).** Execution stops at the **first** failing call or limit in the canonical order; its row is the result (C08 limit, C09 timeout, C10 git error, C11 patch-id, C13 hook/direct I/O). Nothing after it runs, so a second injected failure cannot change the result. No implementation may reorder reads to reach a different first failure.
- **Failure mapping (unchanged):** a non-zero exit of any git call → **C10** (§1.3), including `cat-file blob` and `ls-tree` used by any read above. **C13** arises only from a direct file read that is not a git call (none occurs in FINALIZE) or from a **[harness] phase hook** that raises an I/O error on entry to a named phase. Every TF-injection fixture names the exact git call or hook (FX-C13, FX-C13b, FX-C13c, FX-K28h, FX-C10b, FX-C10c, FX-C10d, FX-C09b, FX-C09c, FX-C10e, FX-C10f).

**Anchoring scope AS (UR-19).** AS is a subset of C's relevant paths.
- **Option (a) (interim):** AS = every relevant path, for ancestry-merge and squash. For ancestry-direct, AS = ∅.
- **Option (b) (R15Δ, R14-05):** AS = the relevant **count-landing-eligible** paths of a **squash** C, and only if UR-21 Option A is adopted. A path is count-landing-eligible iff it is landing-textual, r ≠ e, it has a **touched line** (survival class S(i)), and C's surviving change at p includes no mode change (S(iii)) (R20Δ: replaces "not relevant only by (iv)"; a relevant path without a touched line is never eligible) — exactly the paths that Option A could land by counts (§1.7.3 Step 2). For ancestry-merge, ancestry-direct, any root C, and whenever UR-21 Option B is in force, AS = ∅ and nothing is read to compute it. (Under Option A, r, e, the kinds and the mode change are read for this — not decided — before anchoring.) Paths that are relevant but not eligible (mode changes, (iv)-only paths, non-textual states) are landed only if r = e and are otherwise unverifiable; anchoring adds nothing there, so they are out of scope (FX-K31o).
- **Option (b′) (R16Δ, R15-05):** AS = ∅ for every C (no anchoring at all). A separate owner option; not chosen.
- **Option (c):** unspecified; (a) applies.

**Anchoring — runs only if AS ≠ ∅.** **K34 and K32 belong to anchoring and nothing else.** If AS = ∅, AN = ANCHORED, Ht and Fk are not computed, and **neither K34 nor K32 can occur**.
1. **np(C) = 0** (a root C; only reachable through a merge, because an absent root C gives K06 first): Fk := ∅ without a git call → **K34**. (`merge-base` is never called with a tree.)
2. **Ht:** Bu for ancestry-merge and first-integration squash; for a chained squash, the single member of `merge-base --all X_v T`; |Ht| ≠ 1 → **K34** (unreachable, L-22; kept as a defensive check).
3. **Fk:** Pre if Ht = Pre; otherwise `merge-base --all Ht Pre`; |Fk| ≠ 1 → **K34**.
4. C is **anchored** iff entry(Ht,p) = entry(Fk,p) for every p ∈ AS. Otherwise → **K32** (base-not-anchored).

**IA7 with several candidates for the same X (R16Δ, R15-06: argument weakened to what follows).** AS is computed per candidate and is **the same for every candidate** in Cmin:
- Candidates in Cmin share pos(M), hence the same M, S, Tb and Tip, and they share X, hence End. Pre and Post are C's.
- **Option (a):** AS = the relevant paths, which depend only on Pre, Post and End → identical.
- **Options (b) × B, (b′) and any UR-21 Option B compilation:** AS = ∅ for every candidate.
- **Option (b) × A:** eligibility also reads the **kinds** of Bu. Option A requires UR-16 with path binding. The candidates' pids are equal, so — under UR-16 — the per-path changes from each Bu_i to X are the same changes, **as line multisets with the same mode and add/delete/binary headers**; patch-id does not hash hunk positions, so **the entries of Bu1 and Bu2 at a path may differ** (the same line missing from two different identical blocks; V-UR16 (d)). What does follow: at every path, Bu1 and Bu2 have the **same kind** (the same text/binary/mode/presence headers, or no change and then both equal X), and the **same per-line counts** (Bu_i(L) = End(L) − Δ(L) with the same Δ). Eligibility reads only kinds, r, e and C's own changes → AS is identical. The **guard** (Tb(L) vs Bu(L)) and the **carried check** (End(L) − Bu(L)) read only counts → identical too.

**AN can still differ between candidates — only through Ht** (FX-K25c: Ht = M1 for one, F0 for the other), because anchoring compares **entries** of Ht and Fk. If the candidates' AN results (ANCHORED / K32 / K34; an empty AS counts as ANCHORED) differ → **K25**; otherwise select the minimum (OID(seg_base), OID(S)).

**Behaviour [R]**

| Situation | Result |
|---|---|
| Ht ⊑ Pre (the usual case; direct always) | Fk = Ht, so the equality is **trivially true**. That is not evidence that C landed. |
| Earlier branch commits touched C's paths (C0 then C on f) | No effect. Fk is a fork point, and entry(Fk,f) is compared with entry(Ht,f), not with Pre. |
| **An earlier squashed segment of the same branch changed a relevant path** (chained, R11-04) | **No effect.** Ht is the target state that segment absorbed, not the segment end X_v. FX-K23al. |
| An unrelated back-merge after C's fork (target changes on other paths only) | Anchored. FX-K23ag. |
| A back-merge after C's fork whose absorbed target changes touched one of C's relevant paths — **including a target squash commit that carries the branch's own earlier segment** (FX-K25c) | Entries differ → **K32** (L-20). This happens even when r = e (FX-K23ai). |

**What anchoring does and does not do (R12Δ, R11-10, R11-11).**
- It never establishes landing. Where r = e, C's surviving state at p is in Tip whatever Bu is, so anchoring adds no landing evidence there.
- Its role is an **attribution guard**: it refuses to credit C when the unit base absorbed target changes on C's paths after C's fork (target pre-emption, the R9-01 constructions).
- Under the interim landing rule (state equality only) anchoring is not needed for soundness. Under UR-21 Option A, the target-count guard below closes the vacuous-removal hole that Revision 11 relied on anchoring to close (table below). Whether to keep anchoring universal is UR-19.

**Step 2 — Landing (runs only if AN = ANCHORED; R15Δ)**

Landing — the type guard and every landing table below — **executes only when AN = ANCHORED** (including AN = ANCHORED because AS = ∅). When AN is K34 or K32, landing is **skipped**: it reads nothing and raises nothing (FX-C13b).

Every relevant p is classified into exactly one of: **landed**, **result-differs**, **unverifiable**, **not-carried**. Let **ub** = entry(Bu,p), t = entry(Tb,p), e = entry(End,p), r = entry(Tip,p) (R13Δ: ub replaces the Revision 12 name b).

**Precedence (R13Δ, R12-05).** Mode is decided first; then, for ancestry-merge and squash only, the type guard; then the mode's table. Direct mode has no type guard.

**ancestry-direct.** Tip = End = C. Every relevant p is **landed by construction**, whatever its kinds (a file replaced by a directory, a symlink, a gitlink or a binary included). No entry comparison is made. `landing=by-construction`.

**Type guard (ancestry-merge and squash only).** If any of entry(Pre,p), entry(Post,p), ub, t, e or r has type `tree`, p is **unverifiable**.

**ancestry-merge.** `landing=by-path-entry`. Line counts are never used for merges.

| Condition | Class |
|---|---|
| r = e | **landed** (state equality: the merge result equals the branch end at p) |
| r ≠ e ∧ t = ub | **result-differs** |
| r ≠ e ∧ t ≠ ub | **unverifiable** |

**squash — interim (UR-21 open; this is Option B).** `landing=by-patch-identity` (the integration was identified by patch identity; landing itself is by state).

| Condition | Class |
|---|---|
| r = e | **landed** (state equality) |
| r ≠ e | **unverifiable** |

This mirrors ancestry-merge, where r ≠ e is never landed. Every squash path whose integrated file differs from the branch end gives K31 (cost: L-21).

**squash — only if UR-21 Option A is adopted (R12Δ, R11-01, R11-02).** Same `landing` token.

| Condition | Class |
|---|---|
| r = e | **landed** (state equality) |
| r ≠ e, and p is **not landing-textual** (R13Δ), or C's surviving change at p includes a mode change (S(iii)), or p has **no touched line** (R20Δ: S(iv)-only, or relevant with no survival class — the guard and the carried check would otherwise pass vacuously) | **unverifiable** |
| r ≠ e, p landing-textual, and the **target-count guard** fails: some touched line L has Tb(L) ≠ Bu(L) | **unverifiable** |
| r ≠ e, p landing-textual, guard holds, and the **carried-by-unit check** holds | **landed** (count-level) |
| r ≠ e, p landing-textual, guard holds, carried check fails | **not-carried** |

**Carried-by-unit check (Option A), for every line L at p:**
- **Additions:** S_A(L) ≤ max(0, End(L) − Bu(L)).
- **Removals (R14Δ, R13-01):** S_R(L) ≤ max(0, Bu(L) − End(L)).

**Why this is the carried condition (R14Δ).** "Carried by the integration unit" means the unit's own net diff Bu → End contains C's surviving change. Per line, that diff changes L's count by End(L) − Bu(L). It contains C's surviving addition iff End(L) − Bu(L) ≥ S_A(L), and C's surviving removal iff Bu(L) − End(L) ≥ S_R(L). Revisions 11–13 used min(S_R(L), Bu(L)) on the left, which exempted the part of a removal exceeding Bu(L). That exemption accepted removals the unit's diff does not contain (R13-01: C removes a line only its predecessor in the unit added; Bu(L) = 0 gave 0 ≤ 0). For a touched line S_A(L) ≥ 1 or S_R(L) ≥ 1, so **neither inequality can now hold vacuously**: each requires the unit's diff to change L by at least one copy in C's direction (§11.26).

**What Option A establishes, and what it cannot [R, UR-16].**
- **Identity I-16.** If UR-16 holds, patch-id equality means S's diff has the same per-file changes, at the same paths, as the unit's diff Bu → End. Applying it to Tb = S^1 changes each line count at p by exactly End(L) − Bu(L): **Tip(L) − Tb(L) = End(L) − Bu(L)**.
- **Guard.** If Tb(L) = Bu(L) for every touched L, then by I-16 **Tip(L) = End(L)** for every touched L: on the lines C's surviving change touches, the integrated state and the branch end hold equal counts. Other lines at p may differ (that is why r ≠ e).
- **Guard cost (R13Δ, R12-03).** The guard also rejects correct integrations. It fails whenever the target, before the squash, changed the count of **any** line that C's surviving change touches, **anywhere in the file** — not only near C's change. Lines that are common in a file trip it easily. Example: C adds `</div>` and `<p>x</p>` to a Blade file; before the squash the target edits a different block of the same file and adds one `</div>`. Then Tb(`</div>`) = Bu(`</div>`) + 1, the guard fails, p is unverifiable and the result is K31, although C's lines are in Tip. The same holds for `}`, `});`, `@endif` and `return;`. **How often this happens is unknown and unmeasured.**
- **Carried (R14Δ).** The unit's own diff changes each touched L by at least C's surviving change, in C's direction — additions and removals alike, with no exemption. This is an attribution condition: it refuses to credit C when the unit's diff does not itself contain the change.
- **Equivalence (R11-01, stated plainly).** By I-16 the guard is exactly "Tip(L) = End(L) for every touched L", and the carried check is exactly a count test on S's own diff: S_A(L) ≤ max(0, Tip(L) − Tb(L)) and S_R(L) ≤ max(0, Tb(L) − Tip(L)). **Option A's landing evidence is therefore entirely count-level.** Patch identity contributes only (1) the selection of the integration and (2) identity I-16, which lets the counts be read off Bu → End.
- **It cannot establish:** which copy of L is C's (L-4); whether an equal count came from C or from an independent equivalent change on the target (L-5); reorderings or whitespace-only changes on the same path (L-23); anything at all if UR-16 fails.

**Which check catches which case (R12Δ, R11-10 corrected).** Derivations in §11.

| Case | Anchoring | Guard (Option A) | Carried (Option A) | Interim (Option B) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R10-01 squash, FX-K31j: C re-adds L that the target removed | trivially true | **catches it**: Tb(L) = 0 ≠ Bu(L) = 1 | catches it: End(L) − Bu(L) = 0 < S_A(L) = 1 | unverifiable (r ≠ e) → K31 |
| **R11-02, FX-K31h:** C removes a branch-local L that the target independently added | trivially true | **catches it**: Tb(L) = 1 ≠ Bu(L) = 0 | catches it too (R14Δ): S_R(L) = 1 > Bu(L) − End(L) = 0 | unverifiable → K31 |
| R9-01 A, FX-K32b: a back-merge absorbed the target's removal of L; the target later restored L | **catches it** (K32) | catches it: Tb(L) = 1 ≠ Bu(L) = 0 | catches it too (R14Δ): S_R = 1 > Bu − End = 0 | K32 (anchoring runs first) |
| R9-01 B, FX-K32d: a back-merge absorbed the target's addition of L; the target later removed it | **catches it** (K32) | catches it: Tb(L) = 0 ≠ Bu(L) = 1 | catches it: End(L) − Bu(L) = 0 < S_A(L) = 1 | K32 |
| Cancel within the unit, FX-K33b: C undoes a predecessor's addition | trivially true | passes: Tb(L) = Bu(L) = 1 | **fails** → not-carried (cost) | unverifiable → K31 |
| **R13-01, FX-K33c:** C removes a line that only its predecessor in the unit added; the target edited another line | trivially true | passes: Tb(L) = Bu(L) = 0 | **catches it** (R14Δ): S_R(L) = 1 > Bu(L) − End(L) = 0 → not-carried. Revision 13 passed it vacuously: min(1, 0) = 0 ≤ 0 → MERGED | unverifiable → K31 |
| Duplicate removal, one copy the predecessor's, FX-K33d | trivially true | passes: Tb(L) = Bu(L) = 1 | **catches it** (R14Δ): S_R = 2 > Bu − End = 1. Revision 13: min(2, 1) = 1 ≤ 1 → landed | unverifiable → K31 |

**R14Δ.** Under Option A the guard catches every case where the target changed the count of a touched line; the corrected carried check catches every case where the unit's own diff does not contain C's per-line change, including the whole R13-01 class. **No check passes vacuously any more.** Anchoring adds attribution conservatism at a cost (L-20). (Revision 11's table wrongly said the carried check misses R9-01 B — R11-10; Revision 13's carried check passed R9-01 A, R11-02, R13-01 and FX-K33d vacuously — R13-01.)

**Result of Step 2** (only if anchored):

| Condition | Row |
|---|---|
| any result-differs | **K30** |
| else any unverifiable | **K31** |
| else any not-carried | **K33** (reachable only under UR-21 Option A) |
| else | landed (continue to events and content) |

The reason token of K31, `merge-content-unverifiable`, is historical; it applies to every mode.

**Unrelated evidence cannot mask a relevant path** (FX-K31l, R13Δ). Landing is decided per relevant path, and a single non-landed path decides K30, K31 or K33 before events and content. A landed path elsewhere (for example the g change that let the integration be found) never offsets it.

**paths_unlanded:**
- numerator = #result-differs + #unverifiable + #not-carried;
- denominator = #relevant paths;
- the four classes are disjoint, and each relevant path has exactly one class;
- **0/0 is permitted.** It means no relevant path, in which case content state 1 or 2 decides (K20 or K21), never MERGED.

**K30's meaning.** The merge result at p differs from the branch end while the target side was unchanged. Possible causes: C's change was dropped (`-s ours`); the merger added a fix-up line (an evil merge); a sibling commit's change at p was dropped. The reason token is `merge-result-differs` (L-19).

**K33's meaning (Option A only).** For squash: the target changed p, the target-count guard holds, and the integration unit's net diff does not carry C's surviving change, for example because C undid a predecessor's change within the same unit (FX-K33b), C removed a line that only a predecessor in the unit had added (FX-K33c, R14Δ), or C removed more copies of a duplicated line than the unit's diff removes (FX-K33d). The reason token is `change-not-carried`.

**Withdrawn.** All count-based landing formulas of Revisions 7–9; the Revision 10 claim that `Bu ⊑ Pre` establishes landing; the Revision 11 squash text rule as **in-force** behaviour (it survives only inside Option A, with the guard added); the Revision 11 claim that the squash rule used "neither counts nor patch identity alone".

#### 1.7.4 Content (current-state) checks

These run after anchoring, landing and events. They compare C's surviving change with Tn. **Net counts, no provenance.**

- **C-text and C-opaque paths:** as defined in §1.7.3 (Pre and Post only). For C-opaque content changes, B and superseded use the survival key and b uses content or kind (R20Δ, R19-03: not entries). Line counts X(L) are taken only on textual states (§1.7.3).
- **C-text path with a non-textual End (R13Δ):** S_A(L) = S_R(L) = 0 for every L by definition (no text change survived). Its A and R count toward superseded.
- **A path with survival class S(i) and a non-textual Tn (R13Δ; R21Δ, R20-01: S(i), not relevance):** C's surviving lines cannot be counted in the current target, so the content state is **insufficient → K22** (applied in states 3–5). A relevant path without S(i) has no surviving C line and never triggers this rule. Tn(L) is never computed on a non-textual state.
- **Text path:** drop whitespace-only lines and compare byte-exact. X(L) is the count of L in blob(X, p); ⊥ counts as 0.
- **Mode-only changes and uncounted changes** (§1.7.3 (iv)) contribute nothing to the quantities below. They are checked by landing only. **A reordering after integration is not detected** (L-23).

| Quantity | Definition |
|---|---|
| A, R | max(0, Post − Pre), max(0, Pre − Post) |
| S_A, S_R | min(A, max(0, End − Pre)), min(R, max(0, Pre − End)) |
| superseded | Σ(A − S_A) + Σ(R − S_R) + C-opaque **content changes** (content(Post) ≠ content(Pre)) whose **content does not survive** (content(End) ≠ content(Post); for a tree result, kind) — also when their kind survives (R21Δ, UR-22 interim) |
| k / K | Σ min(S_A, max(0, Tn − Tb)) / ΣS_A [B-18] |
| j_abs / J | Σ{S_R(L) : Tn(L) = 0} / ΣS_R |
| j_inc / J | Σ min(S_R, max(0, Tn − Tip)) / ΣS_R |
| b / B | B = C-opaque content changes that survive under the UR-22 rule in force (interim: content survives, or kind-changing with kind surviving); b = members of B whose current target state matches the branch end: kind(Tn) = tree when C's result is a directory, otherwise content(Tn) = content(End) (R21Δ, R20-03/R20-04). **B\*** = members of B whose content survives (used only by state 2). |

**Content state** (first match):

| # | Condition | State | content_basis |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | ΣA + ΣR + #(C-opaque content changes) = 0 | unassessable (**K20**) — a mode-only change, text or binary, lands here (R18Δ; FX-K20c, FX-K20e) | `-` |
| 2 | K + J + B\* = 0 (R21Δ: B\* under the UR-22 interim; = B under Options A and B) | superseded [P-06 · PDB; if rejected → insufficient] (**K21**) — means no **counted or opaque content** change of C survives; a mode change ((iii)) or an uncounted change ((iv)) may still survive and have landed, and the reason does not claim otherwise (R19Δ, R18-08) | `-` |
| 3 | K > 0 | sufficient iff k = K ∧ b = B (j_abs, j_inc reported, not required [UR-14]) | `added` |
| 4 | K = 0, J > 0 | sufficient iff j_inc = 0 ∧ b = B [P-07 · PDB; if rejected: insufficient] | `removed` |
| 5 | otherwise | sufficient iff b = B | `opaque` |

Insufficient → **K22**. **R13Δ; R21Δ:** in states 3–5 the state is also insufficient if any path **with survival class S(i)** has a non-textual Tn.

**Zero relevant paths.** If C has no relevant path, landing is vacuous, and content state 1 (K20) or state 2 (K21) decides. MERGED never results. A C whose only change is a mode change or an uncounted change (including adding an empty file, FX-K20d) reaches state 1 (K20) even when landed.


### 1.8 Integration algorithm

#### 1.8.1 Present C

```
if np(C) ≥ 3:                                  K02   [P-16 · PDB: K02; if rejected: proceed]
P := oldest X ∈ FP with C ⊑ X
if P = C:      I := (ancestry-direct, base=parent0(C), tip=C, end=C, integrating=C, merge=-,
                     seg_base=-, lineage_root=C, landing=by-construction)
elif np(P) ≥ 3:                                K05
elif np(P) = 2:
     require C ⊑ P^2 ∧ C ⋢ P^1                 else raise C12
     I := (ancestry-merge, base=P^1, tip=P, end=P^2, integrating=P, merge=P, seg_base=-,
           lineage_root=C, landing=by-path-entry)
else:          raise C12
→ FINALIZE(I)
```

If P = C and np(C) = 2, C's change is diff(C^1, C).

#### 1.8.2 Absent C

| Row | Condition |
|---|---|
| K03 | np(C) = 2 |
| K04 | np(C) ≤ 1 ∧ paths(C) = ∅ |
| K06 | NB := `merge-base --all C T`; \|NB\| = 0 |
| K07 | \|NB\| > 1 |

**IA1 (endpoints)**
- refs := `for-each-ref --contains C refs/remotes/origin/`, minus `refs/remotes/origin/{development,HEAD}` [P-04 · PDB: remote-tracking refs only; if adopted: plus `refs/heads/`].
- E := `rev-list C <refs…> --not T`.
- ord := rank in a parents-before-children Kahn order on E (smallest OID first among ready nodes), counted from 0.

**Endpoint check.** For each X ∈ E in ascending ord, let n := |`merge-base --all X T`|. The first X with n ≠ 1 decides:
- n = 0 → **K27**;
- n > 1 → **K08** [P-02 · PDB: K08; if rejected: drop X and continue].

Otherwise B_X is that single merge base.

**IA2 (target-side candidates)**
- RANGE := `rev-list T --not NB`.
- SSHAPE := GitLab pairs with M ∈ FP ∩ RANGE.
- SOTHER := non-merge commits of RANGE that are not the S of any SSHAPE pair.
- PMAP_S: pid(parent0(S), S) → pairs, ordered by pos(M).
- PMAP_O: pid → commits, ordered by OID.
- NULL pids are dropped.

**IA3–IA4 (first-integration matches)**
- For X ∈ E: p := pid(B_X, X).
- (S, M) ∈ PMAP_S[p] is kept iff B_X ⊑ S^1 and the pair predicates hold. It is added to V with **seg_base = B_X**.
- Each S′ ∈ PMAP_O[p] gives a **shape hit** (X, B_X, S′).

**IA5 (chaining)**
- For X2 ∈ E in ascending ord, and for each v ∈ V with X_v ⊑ X2 and X_v ≠ X2, in descending (pos(M_v), ord(X_v)): accept (S2, M2) ∈ PMAP_S[pid(X_v, X2)] iff M_v ⊑ S2^1, pos(M2) > pos(M_v), and v is the maximal previous entry.
- An accepted pair is added to V as (X2, **seg_base = X_v**, S2, M2).

**IA6 (covering).** (X, base, …) covers C iff C ⊑ X ∧ C ⋢ base. Here base = seg_base for V entries and B_X for shape hits.

**IA7 (selection)** — R14Δ: AN per candidate, each with its own in-scope set AS (UR-19; §1.7.3)
1. Cmin := the covering V entries with minimal pos(M).
2. If Cmin has more than one distinct X → **K25** [P-24 · PDB: K25; (a): max brank, then min ord(X), then OIDs; (b): min ord(X), then OIDs; if rejected: K25]. Here brank = 0 if seg_base ∉ E, else 1 + ord(seg_base).
3. If Cmin has one distinct X but more than one seg_base:
   - compute AN (§1.7.3) for each candidate, each with its own AS (an empty AS gives ANCHORED); AS is identical across these candidates (§1.7.3), so AN can differ only through Ht;
   - if the candidates do not all have the same anchoring result (anchored / K32 / K34) → **K25**;
   - otherwise select the minimum (OID(seg_base), OID(S)).
4. If Cmin has a single entry, select it.
5. I := (squash, base=S^1, tip=M, end=X, integrating=S, merge=M, **seg_base**, lineage_root=S, landing=by-patch-identity).

**No covering V entry**

| Row | Condition |
|---|---|
| K10 | a shape hit covers C |
| K11 | refs = ∅ |
| K12 | cand ≠ ∅ |
| K13 / K14 | otherwise |

cand := `rev-list --no-merges --full-history T --not NB -- paths(C)` ∪ {merges G ∈ RANGE with non-empty `diff-tree --cc --name-only -z` on paths(C)} \ {S_v, M_v : v ∈ V}.

#### 1.8.3 FINALIZE

**Compute first (R15Δ, R14-01), in this order:** (1) Bu (structural, ancestry-merge only); (2) AS, then AN (anchoring over AS; Ht and Fk only if AS ≠ ∅); (3) LD — **only if AN = ANCHORED**, otherwise skipped; (4) EV (§6), PR and CK — **always**, whatever AN is. A C row raised by any computation that runs preempts every K row. A skipped computation raises nothing. **R16Δ (R15-02):** if (1) gives K28 or K29, (2) and (3) are not computed (AS, Ht, Fk and LD; AN := BU_NONE/BU_MULTI) and (4) still runs; every computation that runs reads its whole read set (§1.7.3 read discipline). **R17Δ (R16-03, R16-05):** AN runs in two stages (merge-base decisions, then entries only if Ht and Fk are single); all reads follow the canonical order and the first failure decides (§1.7.3); IA7 step 3 (candidate anchoring) is anchoring, not Step 0. **R18Δ (R17-04):** the full sequence is §1.7.3's canonical order — (IA7 step 3 when applicable, candidates by (OID(seg_base), OID(S))) → Bu → relevance → AS → AN stages → LD (only if anchored) → EV (§6.1, §6.2) → PR → CK → DG; EV, PR, CK and DG always run; the first failing call decides.

**Then decide, in this order:**
1. K28, K29 (ancestry-merge Bu count — **structural**, every UR-19 option)
2. **K34** (Ht or Fk undetermined — **anchoring**; only if AS ≠ ∅)
3. **K32** (not anchored — **anchoring**; only if AS ≠ ∅)
4. K30, K31, **K33** (landing)
5. K15, K16, K17, K18, K26 (events)
6. K19
7. K20, K21, K22
8. K23 or K24

**Definitions used here**
- **PR** := #{p ∈ paths(C) : entry(I.base,p) ≠ entry(I.tip,p) ∧ entry(T,p) = entry(I.base,p)}.
- **Diagnostics:**
  - `drift` := #{p ∈ paths(C) : entry(T,p) ≠ entry(End,p)};
  - `later_commits` := `rev-list --count T --not I.tip -- paths(C)`;
  - `c_paths_in_unit` := |paths(C) ∩ paths(IU)|.

Any C row raised during the computations preempts every K row.

#### 1.8.4 Evidence table

| via | base | tip | end | seg_base | Bu | landing | attribution |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ancestry-direct | parent0(C) | C | C | - | Pre | by-construction | graph |
| ancestry-merge | P^1 | P | P^2 | - | merge-base(P^1, P^2) | by-path-entry | graph |
| squash | S^1 | M | X | B_X or X_v | seg_base | by-patch-identity (integration); landing by state equality, or Option A | content-equivalent |

Ht (§1.7.3) is Pre for direct, Bu for ancestry-merge and first-integration squash, and merge-base(X_v, T) for a chained squash.


### 1.9 Outcomes — R12Δ (R11-05, R11-01)

**B-08 verbatim** (owner's Revision 4 and Revision 5 briefs):
- "MERGED means historical integration established under the defined evidence rules, with no exact revert detected by the defined checks. It does not prove deployment, completion, or uninterrupted present-day existence."
- "`MERGED` means historical integration was detected and no exact revert was detected; it does not prove current presence, unchanged state, deployment, or completion."

These are applied literally. Revert-then-reapply → K26 [P-22 needs an explicit B-08 amendment].

| Outcome | Meaning |
|---|---|
| MERGED | (1) An integration of C into T's history was detected, by graph or by patch identity. (2) **Anchoring, only within its scope AS (R15Δ, R14-02):** for every path in AS, the unit's absorbed-target base agrees with the fork point. Under UR-19 option (a), AS is every relevant path (for merges and squashes). Under option (b), AS holds only the squash paths eligible for count landing, and may be empty; under option (b′) (R16Δ) it is always empty; **for paths outside AS nothing is claimed** — in particular, MERGED does not then establish that the target did not make the same change first and the branch absorb it (attribution; §2.3 UR-19 (b)). (3) **For every relevant path** — every path C changed whose branch-end entry differs from C's parent's (R20Δ; this includes every path where any part of C's change survives, and also paths the branch changed again after C) — **Tip agrees with the branch end at that path by state equality**; or, **only if UR-21 Option A is adopted**, for squash text paths, by equal net line counts on every line C's surviving change touches. (4) No exact revert event was detected. (5) Current-state net line counts are consistent. It does **not** establish: line provenance; which copy of a line is C's; that an equal count came from C rather than from an independent equivalent change; that a reordering survived after integration; current presence of C's specific lines; intactness; deployment; completion; effective transport; canonical-upstream identity. |
| LIVE | No supported integration; no foreign edit since NB; target fresh with respect to the repository-local configured origin. |
| STALE_REFERENCE | As LIVE, but the target is stale or its freshness unknown. |
| GONE | C is absent locally. |
| AMBIGUOUS(reason) | Classification refused. |
| TOOL_FAILURE(code) | Execution failure. |

### 1.10 Recommendation and caveats — R12Δ

| Result | recommend | Wording |
|---|---|---|
| MERGED, fresh=yes | yes | "Integrated into origin/development (observed <observed_at>, fresh); no exact revert detected; presence, deployment and completion are not established." [P-18] |
| MERGED, fresh=no, observed_at ≠ none | historical | **"Integrated as observed at <timestamp>; target is stale; later reverts are not verified."** [B-09] |
| MERGED, observed_at=none | no | report only [B-19] |
| LIVE | yes | "No integration of this commit found in origin/development as observed at <observed_at> (fresh); this does not establish that the work is unfinished, wanted, or undeployed." [P-18] |
| AMBIGUOUS / STALE_REFERENCE / GONE | no | report only [B-17] |
| TOOL_FAILURE | (no field) | "merge-status failed: <code>" |

**Caveat channel.** The helper emits `caveats=` as an ascending list, or `-`. Observe maps each token to its text, filling placeholders only from the same line. Observe never computes a caveat condition.

**Caveat rules** [texts P-21 · PDB: these texts; if rejected: owner texts, same tokens and conditions]

| Token | Emitted iff | Text |
|---|---|---|
| CV1 (R20Δ, R19-02) | outcome=MERGED ∧ content_basis ∈ {added, removed} | One text is compiled in, chosen by the owner's UR-19 and UR-21 answers; the helper never chooses at run time. **Covered files** (= the relevant paths, §1.7.3, stated as the rule states them): "each file this commit changed whose state at the branch end — content, kind or file mode — still differs from its state before this commit, including — when they meet that condition — files this commit added or deleted, files it turned into or out of a directory, link or submodule, and files changed again later on the branch; a file this commit added that the branch later deleted is not covered, because it is absent both before this commit and at the branch end (R21Δ, R20-05); no other file is covered. Covering a file says that its branch-end state was compared, not that this commit's change survives there (changes replaced on the branch are counted by CV3)". **Clause A (anchoring), only under UR-19 (a):** "on the covered files, the target state the branch had absorbed before integration agrees with this commit's fork point" (under (a) the covered files are exactly AS for merges and squashes; for ancestry-direct this holds by construction). **Under (b) and (b′) clause A is omitted** (CV7/CV8 disclose attribution). **Clause L (landing):** under UR-21 Option B — "each covered file's state is reflected in the integrated state by file equality"; under Option A — "… by file equality, or by matching patch plus equal net line counts on this commit's lines (which does not show the lines came from this commit)". Content confirmation is not attribution. **Full text:** "Integration was checked per changed file (<landing>). Covered files: <covered files>. [<clause A>.] <clause L>. Current-state checks are net line counts per file, do not detect reordering, and do not establish which lines are this commit's (added present <added_lines_present>, removed absent <removed_lines_absent>)." Interim compilation: (a) × B. |
| CV2 | MERGED ∧ j_inc > 0 | "Since integration, the target gained at least <j_inc> copy/copies (net, counted per file) of line(s) this commit removed; this does not establish who reintroduced them, and the change may not be intact." |
| CV3 | MERGED ∧ superseded > 0 | "<superseded> of this commit's changes were replaced on its own branch before integration." |
| CV4 | snapshot ∈ {files-only, none, final-unreadable} | "Repository state was not fully compared (snapshot=<snapshot>); stability is not established." |
| CV5 | MERGED ∧ a revert event exists (only if P-22 adopted) | "An exact revert of this change was detected and later re-applied (events=<events>)." |
| CV6 | recommend ∈ {yes, historical} ∧ origin_identity = unpinned | "The target and its freshness refer to the origin URL in this repository's local configuration only (class <origin_url_class>); global or system configuration, environment settings at fetch time, SSH host aliases, name resolution and URL changes since the fetch are not inspected, and canonical-upstream identity is not verified." |
| **CV7** (R20Δ, R19-04) | outcome=MERGED ∧ UR-19 compiled as (b) or (b′) ∧ via ≠ ancestry-direct ∧ **not CV8** | "Attribution was not checked: the target state absorbed by the branch was not compared with this commit's fork point for any file; if the target made an equivalent change first and the branch absorbed it, this result would look the same." Covers every (b) × B and (b′) MERGED, every (b) × A merge, and every (b) × A squash whose AS is empty. Not emitted for ancestry-direct (attribution holds by construction there) or under (a)/(c). |
| **CV8** (R20Δ, R19-04) | outcome=MERGED ∧ UR-19 compiled as (b) ∧ UR-21 = Option A ∧ via = squash ∧ **AS ≠ ∅** (decided at run time from the same line's evaluation) | "Attribution was checked only in part: the target state absorbed by the branch was compared with this commit's fork point only for the files whose integrated content was confirmed by line counts (at least one), and for no other file; if the target made an equivalent change first and the branch absorbed it on another file, this result would look the same." (No new output field: the helper chooses CV7 or CV8 from AS, which it has computed for the same line.) |

**Which caveats can appear, by line type**

| Line | Possible caveats |
|---|---|
| MERGED | CV1, CV2, CV3, CV6 (under PDBs); CV7 or CV8 only under UR-19 (b)/(b′) |
| LIVE | CV6 |
| STALE / GONE / AMBIGUOUS | none |
| TF | CV4 iff not `full` |


### 1.11 Output grammar (ABNF)

```
line        = classified / failure
classified  = "outcome=" OUTCOME SP "reason=" (REASON / "-") SP "trace=" KTRACE
              SP "fresh=" ("yes" / "no") SP "observed_at=" (TS / "none")
              SP "freshness_source=" ("reflog" / "fetch_head" / "both" / "none")
              SP "freshness_trace=" RROW "/" FROW "/" CBROW
              SP "freshness_reason=" FREASON SP "fetch_head=" FHSTAT
              SP "origin_identity=" ("unpinned" / "pinned" / "pin-mismatch" / "pin-missing")
              SP "origin_url_class=" OUCLASS
              SP "commit=" HEX40 SP "via=" (VIA / "-") SP "attribution=" (ATTR / "-")
              SP "landing=" (LANDING / "-") SP "paths_unlanded=" (FRAC / "-")
              SP "integrating=" (HEX40 / "-") SP "merge=" (HEX40 / "-")
              SP "segment_base=" (HEX40 / "-") SP "segment_end=" (HEX40 / "-")
              SP "covering=" (NUM / "-") SP "refs=" (REFLIST / "-") SP "events=" (NUM / "-")
              SP "paths_reverted=" (FRAC / "-") SP "added_lines_present=" (FRAC / "-")
              SP "removed_lines_absent=" (FRAC / "-") SP "removed_lines_increased=" (FRAC / "-")
              SP "binary_present=" (FRAC / "-") SP "content_basis=" (BASIS / "-")
              SP "superseded=" (NUM / "-") SP "drift=" (FRAC / "-")
              SP "later_commits=" (NUM / "-") SP "c_paths_in_unit=" (FRAC / "-")
              SP "snapshot=full" SP "caveats=" (CVLIST / "-")
              SP "recommend=" ("yes" / "historical" / "no") LF
failure     = "outcome=TOOL_FAILURE" SP "code=" CODE SP "trace=" XTRACE
              SP "provisional=" (PTRACE / "-") SP "snapshot=" SNAP
              SP "caveats=" (CVLIST / "-") LF
OUTCOME  = "MERGED" / "LIVE" / "AMBIGUOUS" / "STALE_REFERENCE" / "GONE"
REASON   = "unsupported-merge-input" / "empty-commit" / "unsupported-integration-shape"
         / "no-common-history" / "multiple-merge-bases" / "revert-undeterminable"
         / "revert-unordered" / "incomparable-events" / "reverted-after" / "revert-reapplied"
         / "path-reverted" / "content-unassessable" / "superseded-in-branch" / "content-missing"
         / "unreachable" / "touched-without-equivalent" / "integration-selection-ambiguous"
```
