### 11.38 R15-01 — CV1's anchoring clause, regression cases — R16Δ

| Case | Revision 15 clause A (a): "the unit's base agrees with the fork point on this commit's files" | Revision 16 clause A (a): "the target state the branch had absorbed … agrees with this commit's fork point on every file where this commit's change survives to the branch end" |
|---|---|---|
| **FX-K23al** (chained; Bu = X1 = A1 holds f line 10; Ht = F0 = Fk) | **false**: entry(Bu, f) = A1(f) ≠ entry(F0, f) | **true**: the absorbed target state is Ht = F0 = Fk; f is where C's change survives |
| Reviewer's case 2 (g superseded on the branch; Hb edits another line of g; AS = {f}) | **false/unchecked** at g ("this commit's files" includes g) | **true**: g is not a file where C's change survives; f is anchored |
| Any (a) MERGED via merge or first-integration squash | true (Bu = Ht there) | true |
| Direct MERGED | true trivially | true trivially (absorbed state = C's parent = fork point) |
| (b) / (b′) MERGED (K23ai, K23ar, K25c, K34a, K34b) | (b) text did not claim anchoring, but basis=opaque MERGED had no disclosure | no clause A; **CV7** on every MERGED, any basis |

Why Revision 15 failed on FX-K23al: it named the **unit's base** (Bu), but anchoring compares Ht — the target state the base absorbed — with the fork point. For a chained segment, Bu = X_v carries the branch's own earlier changes and is not the target state. Revision 16 names exactly what is compared, on exactly the files compared (AS = relevant paths under (a)).

### 11.39 R15-02 — Step 0 failure continuation — R16Δ

- §1.7.3 ("Step 0 failure continuation"), §1.8.3 ("Compute first", K28/K29 sentence) and §4 (`anchoring()` returns BU_NONE/BU_MULTI before AS; `LD := landing(I) if AN = ANCHORED`) now say the same: after K28/K29, AS, AN and LD are not computed; EV, PR and CK run.
- **No later computation reads the missing Bu:** EV reads I.base, I.tip and §6 records; PR reads I.base, I.tip, T; CK reads Pre, Post, End, Tb (= I.base = P^1), Tip, Tn; relevance reads Pre, Post, End. Bu appears only in AS (via kinds), anchoring (Ht = Bu) and landing (ub), all skipped.
- **FX-K28h:** K28 in every combination; the landing-phase hook never fires. With the hook moved to the content phase instead, the result would be C13 (content runs) — the precedence rule, not an inconsistency.

### 11.40 R15-03 — read discipline and failure mapping — R16Δ

- **Eager vs lazy is no longer open:** every computation that runs reads its whole read set (§1.7.3). The reviewer's case (a K32d variant with J = 0 and a failing `cat-file blob <Tip(f)>`): CK runs (always) and its read set includes blob(Tip, f) for the C-text path f → that git call fails → **C10**, under every option. A lazy implementation that skipped the read is non-conforming.
- **Mapping:** git call failure → C10 (§1.3); harness phase hook or direct file I/O → C13. Revision 15's "C13 (TF io)" for a failed blob read (§11.32 (2)) was wrong; it is C10. Residual (q) is restated.
- **Named injections:** FX-C13 (content-phase hook → C13), FX-C13b and FX-C13c (landing-phase hook), FX-K28h (landing-phase hook, never fires), FX-C10 (C's tree removed → a git call fails → C10).

- **FX-K23ar** (binary change also made by the target and back-merged): (a) → K32; (b)/(b′) → MERGED, basis=opaque, CV6, **CV7**. Revision 15 printed CV6 only.
- CV7's condition is the compiled UR-19 option, not the content basis, so every MERGED under (b)/(b′) carries it (K23ai, K25c, K34a, K34b, K23ar).
- The (b) rationale now says what (b) does: keep anchoring where landing rests on counts (attribution conservatism), drop it where landing is by state or impossible. The previous rationale argued for (b′); (b′) is now offered separately. No option was chosen; (a) remains interim.

**Matrix extension for (b′)** (re-derived; same order as §11.33):

| Fixture | (b′) × A | (b′) × B |
|---|---|---|
| K32a, K32c (merges) | K31 | K31 |
| K32b | **K31** (AS = ∅; guard Tb(L) = 1 ≠ Bu(L) = 0) | K31 |

### 11.42 R15-06 — what AS invariance actually rests on — R16Δ

Equal pids (under UR-16 with path binding) give equal per-path change multisets, modes and headers, **not** equal hunk positions. So Bu1 and Bu2 can differ in entries at a path, but not in kind or in per-line counts. AS ((b) × A) reads kinds, r, e and C's changes; the guard and carried check read counts → identical. Anchoring reads entries of **Ht** (not Bu) → may differ → K25 by IA7. Nothing in the design reads entry(Bu_i, p) across candidates except landing's ub in the merge table, which does not occur for squash candidates. **V-UR16 (d)** asks whether patch-id ignores hunk position (the design assumes it may). UR-21 Option A advice is unchanged: not safe to adopt until V-UR16 (a)–(d) pass.

### 11.43 R15-07 — filing convention, FX-K31o, candidate anchoring — R16Δ

- **Filing convention (R16Δ):** in §3, an option-dependent fixture is listed under **every** row that any of its branches reaches, with the branch named. A fixture counts toward a row's **binding** coverage only if every branch reaches that row (status B, Bo or B(outcome)). FX-K23ai, K31o, C13b, K32a–d, K33b–d, K23aj/am/aq, K25c, K34a/b and K23ar are listed this way.
- **FX-K31o** graph completed (Hr after Hb); all six combinations derived in its row and in §11.41.
- **Candidate anchoring** is classified as anchoring (§1.7.3): it runs before FINALIZE, only where AS ≠ ∅, feeds only K25, and a C row it raises preempts every K row (e.g. a failing `merge-base` → C10).

### 11.45 R16-01 — CV1 clause A against the relevance rules — R17Δ

| Case | Relevant? (rules (i)–(iv)) | Revision 16 clause A ("every file where this commit's change survives") | Revision 17 clause A |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Reviewer's case:** C swaps two lines of g (A = R = 0); a back-merged target edit Hb changes another line of g, so End(g) ≠ Post(g); f anchored and landed | g: no touched line; (iv) fails (End ≠ Post) → **not relevant**; AS = {f} | **false**: C's reorder survives in g, so g was claimed, but entry(Hb, g) ≠ entry(F0, g) | **true**: g's reorder was "later edited on the branch" → explicitly not covered; f is covered and anchored |
| FX-K23al (chained) | f relevant | true (R16 fix) | true |
| Superseded file (C's line change replaced on the branch) | not relevant | true | true ("any change that did not survive is not covered") |
| C's reorder unaltered at End (End = Post) | relevant by (iv) → in AS under (a) | true | true |
| Mode-only, binary, symlink, submodule, file-to-directory change surviving | relevant by (ii)/(iii) | true | true (named) |
| Direct MERGED | AS = ∅; absorbed state = C's parent = fork point | true | true (holds without a check) |

**Claim corrected:** Revision 16 said "those files are exactly AS". That was wrong: a change can survive to the branch end and still not be relevant (an uncounted change later edited on the branch, L-23). Revision 17's clause A enumerates the four relevance rules, which **are** AS under (a).

| (b) × A | exactly the count-confirmed paths (possibly ∅) | "checked only in part … only for files … confirmed by line counts (if any)" | yes |
| (b) × B | ∅ | "not checked … for any file" | yes |

| (b′) × B | ∅ | "not checked …" | yes |
| (c) | compiled as (a) | not emitted | — |

**FX-K23ar** (binary back-merge, r = e): (b) × A → AS = ∅ (r = e) → "(if any)" → none — true; (b) × B, (b′) → "not checked" — true. Caveats CV6, CV7 in every (b)/(b′) cell. **Note (reviewer's point):** under (b′) × A the carried check still catches the case where the target added the same line first and the branch absorbed it (S_A = 1 > End − Bu = 0 → K33), so the attribution loss under (b′) is smaller than "never checked" suggests; CV7 remains conservative. Residual (s) is restated.

### 11.47 R16-03 — staged AN, canonical order, multiple failures — R17Δ

- **FX-C10b** (FX-K34a + Mt2; failing `ls-tree Mt -- f`): (a): AN stage 1 → Fk = {Ka, Kb} → K34; stage 2 (the only reader of Mt's entries) does not run; LD skipped; EV/PR/CK do not read Mt → **K34**, the same in §1.7.3 and §4. (b)/(b′): merges have AS = ∅ → LD runs → reads ub = entry(Mt, f) → **C10**. Both readings now agree because §1.7.3 stages AN exactly as §4's early return does.
- **FX-C10c** (root C): (a) → K34 before any call; (b)/(b′) → C10 via ub.
- **FX-C10d** (two failures): the first failing call in the canonical order decides → C10; the C08 limit at g's Tn is never reached.
- **Precedence is a rule, not an implementation choice:** reads follow the canonical order; the first failure stops execution; no reordering is permitted.

### 11.53 R17-01 — survival by content, re-derived for every change kind — R18Δ

| Change kind (C) | Later branch-side edit | Revision 17 relevance | Revision 18 relevance | Outcome if the target then overwrote it |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| binary content v1 → v2 | `chmod +x` | **dropped** (full entry differs) | (ii) content(End) = (binary, v2) → relevant | r ≠ e → K31/K30 (FX-K31p); target-first + back-merge → K32 (a) / K31 (b) (FX-K31q) |
| binary content | content changed again | not relevant (superseded) | not relevant (superseded; CV3 true) | — |
| symlink target change | — (mode fixed at 120000) | as (ii) | (ii) by (symlink, oid) | r ≠ e → K31 |
| gitlink pointer change | — (mode fixed at 160000) | as (ii) | (ii) by (gitlink, oid) | r ≠ e → K31 |
| file → directory (d → d/x) | a file added under d/ | **dropped** (tree oid differs) | (ii) content = (tree) → relevant | merge/squash: type guard → K31 (FX-K31r; L-26); direct: landed |
| directory → file | — | (ii) | (ii) by (kind, oid) | r ≠ e → K31 |
| text line change | `chmod +x` | (i) relevant (counts) | (i) relevant | r ≠ e → K31 |
| reorder / whitespace-only | `chmod +x` | **dropped** ((iv) compared entries) | (iv) by bytes → relevant | r ≠ e → K31 (FX-K31s) |
| reorder / whitespace-only | content edited elsewhere in the file | not relevant (L-23) | not relevant (L-23; CV1 excludes it by "bytes … exactly this commit's") | not claimed |
| mode-only change (text or binary) | content edited | (iii) relevant (mode survives) | (iii) relevant | r ≠ e → K31 |
| mode-only change on a binary | — | relevant; **B counted it → MERGED basis=opaque** | relevant by (iii); not a content change → state 1 → **K20** (FX-K20e) | — |

**Downstream re-derivation.** B = C-opaque content changes relevant by (ii); b = members of B with content(Tn) = content(Post) (a later target `chmod` does not count as removing C's content; a later target content change does); superseded counts C-opaque content changes with content(End) ≠ content(Post); content state 1 counts C-opaque **content** changes. AS: unchanged in definition ((a) = relevant paths; (b) eligibility excludes mode changes and non-textual states anyway). Landing: unchanged (full entries, r = e). **FX-K31p is a K31, not a MERGED, in every cell.**

**Fail-closed result:** where survival could not be judged safely by content (the directory case), the path is made relevant and the type guard gives K31 for merges and squashes. Cost: L-26.

### 11.54 R17-02 — CV1 states the relevance rules themselves — R18Δ

CV1's covered-files sentence is the text of rules (i)–(iv) in plain words; nothing is paraphrased as "survives". Checks:
- **R16-01 case (FX-K23at):** g's bytes differ at the branch end → not covered → clause A true (only f is covered, and f is anchored).
- **Mixed file** (C adds L and swaps M, N in f; a later branch commit deletes L): (i) fails (no touched line), (iv) fails (A(L) > 0) → f not covered. CV1: f is not "a file where a line … is still present", and not "a text file changed **without** counted lines" → not covered → true.
- **Binary + chmod (FX-K31p/K23as):** covered by "kind and object … exactly this commit's" — consistent with (ii) by content.
- **Directory C created, later extended:** covered ("counts while it is still a directory") — consistent with (ii) by kind.
- **Clause L's antecedent** is now "each covered file" in every cell, including (b)/(b′), where clause A is omitted (R17-06).

### 11.55 R17-03 — FX-C10d re-derived — R18Δ

Sequence (canonical order): relevance reads Pre, Post, End at f then g (End(g) = Post(g), the original blob); AS (none under (a)); AN stage 1 (Ht = Fk = F0, no call beyond those defined); stage 2 reads entry(F0, f), entry(F0, g); **LD at f reads entry(Tip, f) → injected failure (1) → C10**. CK, which alone reads blob(Tn, g) (the distinct 9 MiB blob of Z), is never reached. The argv of (1) is not issued earlier (relevance reads End = C, not Tip = M). Status B: every asserted field is a TF field, independent of open items.

### 11.56 R17-04 — one sequence, every call assigned — R18Δ

| Call or read | Computation | Position |
|---|---|---|
| `merge-base --all P^1 P^2` | Bu (Step 0) | FINALIZE 1st (merges only) |
| entries and blobs of Pre, Post, End | relevance | after Bu (or in IA7 step 3, once) |
| kinds of Bu, Tb, Tip; r, e | AS ((b) × A only) | after relevance |
| `merge-base` for Ht (chained) and Fk | AN stage 1 | after AS |
| entries of Ht, Fk at AS | AN stage 2 | after stage 1, only if both single |
| entries (and under A, blobs) of Pre, Post, Bu, End, Tb, Tip | LD | only if AN = ANCHORED |
| DIFF + patch-id for u_I, u_C, r_I, r_C | EV (§6.1) | first in EV |
| `rev-list T --not I.tip`, record pids | EV (§6.2) | after §6.1 |
| entries of I.base, I.tip, T | PR | after EV |
| entries and blobs of Pre, Post, End, Tb, Tip, Tn | CK | after PR |
| entries of T and End (`drift`); `rev-list --count …` (`later_commits`); `diff-tree` on IU (`c_paths_in_unit`) | DG | after CK, **always** |
| candidates' AS, AN stages | IA7 step 3 | pre-FINALIZE, candidates by (OID(seg_base), OID(S)) |

Fixtures: FX-C09b (Bu before relevance → C09), FX-C09c (candidate order → C09 under (a)), FX-C10e (DG always runs → C10), FX-C10f (§6.1 in EV → C10). §1.7.3, §1.8.3 and §4 state this sequence; PV-27 and PV-30 are restated to claim exactly that.

### 11.61 R18-01 — survival key, re-derived for every transition — R19Δ

| C's change at p | Later branch edit | sk | Revision 18 | Revision 19 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| binary → text (FX-K31t) | edits the text | kind | **dropped** → MERGED + false CV3 | relevant → r ≠ e → K31 |
| symlink → text (FX-K31u) | edits the text | kind | dropped | relevant → K31 |
| gitlink → text (FX-K31v) | edits the text | kind | dropped | relevant → K31 |
| directory → file (FX-K31w) | edits the file | kind | dropped | relevant → type guard → K31 |
| file or anything → directory (FX-K31r) | adds a file under it | kind (tree) | relevant | relevant (unchanged) |
| text → binary, binary → binary, symlink/gitlink changes | changes the object | content | superseded | superseded **by sk** — but (R20Δ, R19-03 correction) this row was wrong for relevance: text → symlink/gitlink were kept relevant only by the mode rule, and text → binary not at all (R19-01). Under Revision 20 every such path is relevant (entry(End) ≠ entry(Pre)) and must land; superseded still counts the replaced object |
| any of the above | `chmod` only | as above | relevant (R17-01 fix) | relevant |
| binary → text, clean squash, later target overwrite (FX-K22j) | edits the text | kind | not in B → MERGED | in B; b needs content(Tn) = content(End) → K22 |

**Downstream.** Relevance: (ii) uses sk. **AS:** (a) = relevant paths, so the R18-01 paths now enter AS; (b) eligibility unchanged (they are not landing-textual: Pre is non-textual). **B** = C-opaque content changes with sk(End) = sk(Post); **b** = members of B whose current target state matches the branch end (kind(Tn) = tree for a directory result, otherwise content(Tn) = content(End)) — for content-keyed paths content(End) = content(Post), so this is Revision 18's test; **superseded** counts C-opaque content changes with sk(End) ≠ sk(Post). **Content state 1** unchanged. **Landing** unchanged: state equality (r = e), type guard for trees. **MERGED** therefore requires, for an R18-01 path, that Tip's entry equals the branch end's — C's surviving change is then in Tip. **Merge and squash**: identical (no count landing is possible for these paths in either UR-21 option, because they are not landing-textual). **Caveats:** CV3 no longer counts these paths; CV1 lists them under item (3).

**Fail-closed cost — L-27 (new identifier; L-26 keeps its meaning).** A non-text object C turned into text, or into a directory, stays relevant while its kind survives, even if a later branch commit rewrote C's text entirely; if the target also changed the path, the result is K31 (or K22 through b), even where a reviewer would call C's change superseded.

**Uncertainty, fail-closed:** a path whose kind changes again later (e.g. binary → text by C, then text → binary on the branch) has sk(End) ≠ sk(Post) → superseded; this is classified deterministically, and the claim made (CV3) is true: C's text result no longer exists at the branch end.

### 11.62 R18-02 — CV1 covered files = the rules — R19Δ

CV1's items (1)–(4) are rules (i)–(iv) with their preconditions in words: (1) = (i) with "text or absent both before and after" and "still text at the branch end"; (2) = (iv); (3) = (ii) with the survival key; (4) = (iii) with R19's both-present definition. Checks: the R18-01 file (binary → text, later edited) is under (3) — "still a text file" — and is relevant, so CV1 neither over- nor under-claims; a binary → text file unchanged at the branch end is under (3) (Revision 18 under-claimed it); a mixed file (counted change removed later, reorder surviving) is under none (as rules (i) and (iv) exclude it); R16-01's file (FX-K23at) is under none. Under (a), the covered files are AS for merges and squashes.

### 11.63 R18-03 — mode change only between two present entries — R19Δ

(iii) now requires entry(Pre,p) ≠ ⊥, entry(Post,p) ≠ ⊥ and entry(End,p) ≠ ⊥. Additions and deletions are judged by (i)/(ii)/(iv) only. Effects: **Option A** — an added or deleted file is no longer barred from count landing as a "mode change"; it still needs landing-textual and r ≠ e (an added file is text-or-absent in every state, so it can be eligible). **AS under (b) × A** — such files can now be in AS, and CV7's (b) × A "count-confirmed files" can include them. **CV1** item (4) covers only permission-bit changes. FX-K20f (true mode change → K20) and FX-K23au (addition and deletion → no (iii)) distinguish the cases. No owner decision is taken: (b) and Option A remain unapproved.

Rewritten in content/sk terms after checking each meaning: FX-K31f (content(End,bin1) = content(Post,bin1) = (binary, v0) ≠ content(Pre,bin1)); FX-K23ak (content(End,bin2) = content(Post,bin2) = ⊥ ≠ content(Pre,bin2)); FX-K23ao (sk = kind: kind(End,d) = kind(Post,d) = tree ≠ content(Pre,d)); §11.23 step 4 (b: kind(Tn,d) = tree). Entry comparisons that remain are deliberate: landing (r = e, t = ub), anchoring (entry(Ht,p) = entry(Fk,p)), PR and drift.

### 11.66 R18-07 — blob-limit scope — R19Δ

The 8 MiB limit applies **only** to `cat-file blob <oid>` reads in the read sets (relevance, AS, LD, CK). Immediately before each such read, at the same position, the helper issues `cat-file -s <oid>` (allowed by §1.3); if the reported size exceeds 8 MiB, the result is **C08** at that position and the blob is not read. DIFF and `patch-id` outputs are bound only by the per-call stdout limit (64 MiB), never by the blob limit. FX-C10d: IA2's DIFF of Z contains the 9 MiB blob but stays under 64 MiB → no failure; CK's `cat-file -s <Tn(g) oid>` would report 9 MiB → C08 — but LD's earlier failure decides first.

- **§11.52** was never written in Revision 18 (numbering jumped from §11.51 to §11.53); it is now present as a short index, so §11 is numbered contiguously.
- **CV7** is compiled per via: "only in part" only for squashes under (b) × A; merges get "not checked".
- **K21's reason** `superseded-in-branch` means: no **counted or opaque content** change of C survives (state 2: K + J + B = 0). A **mode change** ((iii)) or an **uncounted change** ((iv)) of C may still survive and have landed; the reason does not claim otherwise (§1.7.4 note; residual (y)).

### 11.69 R19-01 — relevance re-derived from its definition; the eight required cases — R20Δ

Relevance R(p) := entry(End,p) ≠ entry(Pre,p). Survival classes S(i)–S(iv) as §1.7.3. Merge examples unless stated; "t ≠ ub" means the target changed p.

| # | Case (Pre → Post; End; target; merge result) | R? | AS (a) | Landing | Superseded (sk) | Content / K | CV1 / CV3 / CV6 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | binary v0 → text t1; End t2 (branch edit); Hr binary v3; result v3 (FX-K31t) | yes (t2 ≠ v0) | in | r = v3 ≠ e = t2, t ≠ ub → unverifiable | S(ii) by kind holds → not superseded | — / **K31** | — |
| 2 | text t0 → binary v0 (same mode); End v1; Hr text t3; result t3 (FX-K31x) | yes (v1 ≠ t0) | in | r ≠ e, t ≠ ub → unverifiable | S(ii) fails → superseded 1 | — / **K31** | — |
| 3 | ⊥ → binary v0; End v1; Hr adds text t9; result t9 (FX-K31y) | yes (v1 ≠ ⊥) | in | unverifiable | superseded 1 | — / **K31** | — |
| 3′ | ⊥ → symlink / gitlink, later edit, target adds text | yes | in | unverifiable | superseded 1 | **K31** | — |
| 4 | binary v0 → ⊥ (deletion); End t5 (branch re-add); Hr binary v3; result v3 (FX-K31z) | yes (t5 ≠ v0) | in | r = v3 ≠ e = t5 → unverifiable | sk(End) = (text) ≠ sk(Post) = ⊥ → superseded 1 | **K31** | — |
| 4′ | as 4, but the target did not touch p (result = End = t5) | yes | in | r = e → landed | superseded 1 | MERGED possible; **CV3 true** (deletion replaced by a re-add) | CV1 covers p ("changed again later on the branch") |
| 5 | text → symlink / gitlink; End retargeted; target edits text; result target text (FX-K31aa) | yes | in | unverifiable | superseded 1 (sk = content) | **K31** | — |
| 6a | file → directory d/{x}; later d/y added (FX-K31r) | yes | in | type guard → unverifiable | sk = kind holds | **K31** (L-26) | — |
| 6b | directory → file d; later edit (FX-K31w) | yes | in | type guard → unverifiable | sk = kind holds | **K31** | — |
| 7 | binary v0 → v1; branch restores v0 (End = Pre) (FX-K23av) | **no** | out | — | superseded 1 | from other paths: MERGED possible | **CV3 true** (undone on the branch) |
| 8 | text: C adds L; branch rewrites f (L gone); Hr edits f; conflict → Hr's f (FX-K31ab) | yes | in | unverifiable | S(i) fails → superseded 1 | **K31** (cost L-29) | — |

**The reviewer's candidate, evaluated.** The Revision 19 review proposed content(End,p) ≠ content(Pre,p) for C-opaque paths. Two adjustments were made after checking every case: (1) it is applied to **every** path of C, not only C-opaque ones, and compares **full entries** (so a surviving mode-only change and a later branch `chmod` are also seen) — this makes the "cannot drop out" argument one line long and closes L-23's landing gap; (2) it is used **only** as the relevance (scrutiny) test. **It does not distinguish a surviving C change from a branch-end state that merely differs from Pre** — case 4 (deletion, then re-add) and case 8 (later rewrite) are relevant although C's change did not survive. That is deliberate: being relevant only requires the branch-end state to land; survival is judged separately by S(i)–S(iv) and sk (superseded, B, b, CV3), so no survival claim rests on R. The cost of over-selection is fail-closed K31/K32 (L-29).

**Concepts kept apart.** Relevant (R) — must land. Survives (S(·), sk) — counted by content checks and CV3. Superseded — a C content change with no survival (sk(End) ≠ sk(Post), or S(i) lines lost). Integrated state equals expected — landing (r = e, or Option A counts on S(i) paths). A relevant path may also be superseded (case 4′): it must land, and CV3 counts the replaced change; both statements are true.

**Landing unchanged.** State equality (r = e) for every relevant path; Option A count landing only for landing-textual paths **with a touched line** (S(i)) and no surviving mode change — a relevant path without a touched line can no longer pass the guard and the carried check vacuously.

**Mode change and superseded (R19-03).** A path whose surviving change is a mode change (S(iii)) is relevant and must land. If C also changed the path's content and that content did not survive, the content change counts toward superseded; the mode change does not. CV3 counts content changes only.

### 11.70 R19-02 — CV1 covered files = the relevant paths — R20Δ

CV1's covered files are now stated as the relevance rule itself: "each file this commit changed whose state at the branch end — content, kind or file mode — still differs from its state before this commit". Re-checked: C removes L from f and the branch later deletes f → End(f) = ⊥ ≠ Pre(f) → covered (Revision 19 omitted it); an added or deleted empty file → covered; text → directory (FX-K23ao, K31r) → covered; opaque deletion still absent at End (FX-K23ak) → covered; text → symlink (whole mode) → covered. Under (a), covered = AS for merges and squashes. CV1 adds that covering is not a survival claim (CV3 counts replaced changes).

§1.7.4's "C-opaque paths are compared by entry" is replaced (B and superseded by sk, b by content/kind). §11.61's row for text → symlink/gitlink is corrected: under R20 such paths are relevant by R (no reliance on the mode rule) and superseded if sk(End) ≠ sk(Post). §9's earlier statements "none known to the author" (Revisions 15–19) are historical; each was later contradicted by a review, and §9.00000000 says so.

### 11.72 R19-04 — CV7 / CV8 — R20Δ

CV8 ("checked only in part") is emitted only for a (b) × A squash whose AS ≠ ∅; CV7 ("not checked") in every other (b)/(b′) case, including (b) × A squashes with AS = ∅ (FX-K23ai, K23ar). The helper chooses from AS on the same line; no output field is added. Grammar: CV list adds CV8.

### 11.73 R19-05 — the repeated-calls sequence — R20Δ

The sequence is **every git call the helper issues after snap0a, in issue order**: preflight (§1.4), resolve_target, snap_refs, decide() (K01–K07 checks, including NB = `merge-base --all C T` and paths(C)), IA1–IA7, FINALIZE. Re-derived injections: FX-C09c — V3's Ht argv `merge-base --all <A1> <T>` is first issued by the IA1 endpoint check (NB's argv is `… <C> <T>`, different); the two Fk argvs first occur in IA7 step 3 → unchanged. FX-C10e — `rev-list --count … -- f g` first occurs in DG → unchanged. FX-C10b/c/d, C09b, C10f — none of their argvs is issued by preflight or decide() → unchanged. An injection on paths(C)'s `diff-tree` would fire at decide()'s K04 check (absent C) — no fixture does this.

### 11.74 R19-06 — the 8 MiB blob-read limit — R20Δ

Every blob read (`cat-file -s` then `cat-file blob`) is subject to the 8 MiB limit, and kind detection (the NUL test in the first 8000 bytes) needs a blob read for every regular file of C in Pre, Post and End, and in CK's states for C-text paths. So **any C that touches a file over 8 MiB, in any of those states, ends in C08 (TOOL_FAILURE)**, even though the kind test needs only 8000 bytes: PDFs, images, archives and large generated files are not classifiable. This is fail-closed and user-visible: Observe prints "merge-status failed: limit". Its frequency is unmeasured; the value is an owner choice under P-19 (L-28). Whether git's own binary detection agrees with the 8000-byte rule is **not** claimed; it is V-UR16 (e).

### 11.77 R20-01 — K22 depends on survival, not on relevance — R21Δ

**S(i) re-derived from its definition.** p ∈ paths(C) has survival class S(i) iff: (1) p is **C-text** — Pre and Post are both textual at p (text or absent); (2) **End is textual** at p (text or absent); and (3) p has a **touched line** — some L with S_A(L) + S_R(L) > 0, where S_A(L) = min(A(L), max(0, End(L) − Pre(L))) and S_R(L) = min(R(L), max(0, Pre(L) − End(L))). Exclusions: a C-text path whose End is non-textual has S_A = S_R = 0 by definition (no S(i)); a C-opaque path never has S(i); a relevant path may have no survival class at all.

**Rule (R21Δ).** In content states 3–5, the state is insufficient (K22) if some path **with survival class S(i)** has a non-textual Tn. A relevant path without S(i) never triggers it: none of C's lines survives there, so there is nothing to count. A genuinely missing C contribution still fails closed: if an S(i) line's file is non-textual in Tn, K22 (FX-K22i, K22l).

| Fixture | R | Survival | Tn kind | Content state | Superseded | K | CV1 / CV3 / CV6 / CV7 / CV8 | Landing |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FX-K23aw (case 1) | f, g | f none; g S(i) | text | 3 sufficient | 1 | MERGED K23 | CV1 (f, g), CV3, CV6; (b)/(b′): +CV7, no clause A | both r = e |
| FX-K22i (case 2) | f | S(i) | binary | insufficient | 0 | **K22** | — | r = e |
| FX-K23ax (case 3, R20-01's exact case) | f, g | f none (End binary); g S(i) | f binary | 3 sufficient | 1 | MERGED K23 (R20: K22) | CV1, CV3, CV6 | both r = e |
| FX-K23ay (case 3 variant) | f, g | f none; g S(i) | f binary | 3 sufficient | 1 | MERGED K23 | CV1, CV3, CV6 | both r = e |
| FX-K22l (case 4) | f, g | f S(i); g none | f binary | insufficient | 1 | **K22** | — | both r = e |
| FX-K31ab (case 5, merge) | f, g | f none; g S(i) | — | — | 1 | **K31** every cell (landing first) | — | f unverifiable |
| FX-K31ac (case 5, squash) | — | — | — | — | — | **K12** every cell (no V entry) | — | — |

Modes: the rule reads only C-text classes, counts and Tn, so it is the same in direct, merge, first-integration and chained squash; UR-19 and UR-21 do not change it (they act on anchoring and landing, which precede content).

**What over-selection can add (corrected).** Making more paths relevant can only add **AMBIGUOUS** results — **K25** (larger per-candidate AS in IA7 step 3 under (a) can create an anchoring disagreement), **K30, K31** (landing), **K32, K34** (anchoring) — and **TOOL_FAILURE** rows from the extra reads of those paths (C08, C09, C10). With R21's restriction it no longer adds **K22**. It can never add a MERGED: every added path is an added requirement before MERGED, never a removed one.

### 11.78 R20-04 — paired kind-transition histories — R21Δ

| History | Interim (strict) | UR-22 A (symmetric kind) | UR-22 B (R19–R20 key) |
|---|---|---|---|
| H1: text → binary v0, branch → v1, target untouched, later target → text t8; plus g (FX-K22m) | K22 (b < B) | K22 | **MERGED + CV3** (Tn(p) unchecked) |
| H2: binary → text t1, branch → t2, later target → binary; plus g (FX-K22j) | K22 | K22 | K22 |
| H3: C's only path text → binary, branch edits, Tn = End (FX-K21c) | **K21** | **MERGED** basis opaque | K21 |
| H4: C's only path binary → text, branch edits, Tn = End (FX-K21d) | **K21** | MERGED | MERGED |
| H5: kind change, then branch restores Pre (End = Pre) | not relevant; superseded; CV3 true | same | same |
| H6: kind change, then branch deletes p (End = ⊥) | relevant (⊥ ≠ Pre); kind does not survive; superseded | same | same |
| H7: kind change, then branch re-adds an opaque entry of a third kind | relevant; kind does not survive; superseded | same | same |
| H8: mode-only change of a kind-changed path (e.g. binary chmod) | content and kind unchanged → survives; S(iii) separate | same | same |

**Which approach the specification justifies.** The survival key's own rationale (Revision 19) is that "the identity of the resulting text or directory may change on the branch later without C's change ceasing to exist". That reasoning applies equally to a binary, symlink or gitlink result of a text file, so **A** is the semantically consistent reading. But A and B differ in both directions: A turns H1's MERGED into K22 (stricter) and H3's K21 into MERGED (looser); B does the reverse. No owner decision (P-06, P-21, UR-19, UR-21) settles which survival meaning the owner wants, so the choice is **UR-22**. The **interim** takes, in every history, the stricter of A and B (H1 K22, H3 K21, H4 K21) — it is stricter than Revision 20 in H4 and H1. It does not broaden MERGED anywhere.

**Effects of the interim** on content (B now includes kind-surviving kind-changing paths), superseded (counts every content-replaced change, so CV3 stays true), CV3 (emitted when content was replaced, even if kind survives), K21 (via B\*), K22 (b checks Tn for kind survivors). Existing fixtures: FX-K22j, K31t–w, K31x, K31aa — unchanged outcomes (landing or b decide); FX-K23ap, K23ao, K23ak — unchanged (content survives). No binding fixture moves.

### 11.79 R20-02 — repeated-calls sequence — R21Δ

The **sequence** begins with the **first git call after snap0a** (preflight's `git version`) and **ends with FINALIZE's last call** (DG's `diff-tree` for `c_paths_in_unit`). Inside it, a repeated argv is answered from its first result and not reissued; an argv-keyed injection fails at that argv's first occurrence. **snap1's calls are outside the sequence: they are always issued afresh and never answered from it**, so the final ref record S1r is a fresh read and the (T, RH) comparison detects ref changes for D02 (FX-S3, S6). Calls outside the sequence never answer calls inside it. The definition does not touch L-28 (each `cat-file -s`/`cat-file blob` is still issued once per oid in the sequence) or L-29 (which paths are read). FX-C09b, C09c, C10b–f, C13b/c and K28h were re-derived; none moves.

### 11.80 R20-03 — owner-facing text — R21Δ

Corrected: §2.3 UR-19 (b) eligibility ("landing-textual, r ≠ e, a touched line (S(i)), no surviving mode change (S(iii))") and disclosure ("CV7, or CV8 when AS ≠ ∅ for a (b) × A squash"); the §4 mapping note (same eligibility); P-21's scope ("caveat texts CV1–CV8"); §1.7.4's b/B row ("the C-opaque content changes counted in B under UR-22's interim rule"). Searched: no other §2 question text still uses "not (iv)-only" or "CV7 on every MERGED".

### 11.81 R20-05 — CV1 parenthetical — R21Δ

CV1 now reads "…still differs from its state before this commit — including, when they meet that condition, files this commit added or deleted …; a file this commit added that the branch later deleted is not covered, because it is absent both before this commit and at the branch end". Re-derived: C adds n, branch deletes n → End(n) = ⊥ = Pre(n) → not relevant → not covered. No other CV1 wording claims coverage without the condition.

### 11.82 R20-06 — canonical-order trigger — R21Δ

Item 1 now triggers on facts decided before relevance: **IA7 has several candidates for the same X** (decided by IA7 step 2) **and the compiled options allow a non-empty AS for a squash** — (a) with either UR-21 option, or (b) with Option A; never (b′), never (b) × B. When triggered: relevance (once), then for each candidate in ascending (OID(seg_base), OID(S)): its AS; **its AN stages only if that AS ≠ ∅**. The order is the same as Revision 20's; only the trigger is now decidable.

### 11.83 Regression and cross-reference audit — R21Δ

Re-derived on paper for this revision: FX-K23aw, K23ax, K23ay, K22i, K22l, K31ab, K31ac, K22m, K21c, K21d, K22j, K23ap, K23ak, K23av, K23at (three cells), K31x–aa, K32a–d (six cells, unchanged), C09b, C09c, C10e. **Not re-attacked** in this revision (carried as previously recorded): freshness, OU, snapshot, revert, Option A guard/carried fixtures (FX-K33*, K23aj/am/aq), K34a/b, K25* — no rule they depend on changed. Universal relevance claim: unchanged; scope paths(C) only (a later rename moves C's content out of scope — L-4). Concepts kept separate: relevance (R), survival (S-classes, sk, UR-22), superseded, landing (r = e), integrated-state equality. L-21 reworded: "the branch end equals the unit base at p".

### 11.85 Residual risks the author could not exclude (R21Δ update)

- **(a) UR-16** may not hold for some diff category, or patch-id may not bind paths. This blocks Option A only; the interim rule does not depend on it.
- **(b) L-21 cost under the interim rule** is unmeasured and may be large for SPSM (squash-only [M]). It may make the helper report K31 for most real squash integrations where the target also changed the same files. The owner's UR-21 decision should weigh this. A measurement on real cases needs separate authorisation (RT-10).
- **(c) IA7's anchoring comparison** may produce K25 more often than expected (FX-K25c shows one natural shape).
- **(d) The guard is stricter than necessary** under Option A. It blocks correct cases whenever the target changed the count of any line C touched, anywhere in the file; common lines make this likely (FX-K31n). This is now stated in the UR-21 decision text and L-21. Frequency unmeasured.
- **(e) Rule (iv) and End ≠ Post:** uncounted changes overwritten later on the branch are unchecked (L-23).
- **(f) FX-K23ak** still admits two readings of rule (ii) for a deleted branch-local opaque file. Both give MERGED.
- **(g) The Ht definition** assumes that a back-merged target state is represented by merge-base(X_v, T). If a branch absorbed target content by cherry-picking rather than merging, Ht does not see it, and anchoring passes. Landing is unaffected (it still needs state equality, or the Option A guard).
- **(i) (R13Δ) L-25 cost:** a current target that made one of C's text files non-textual gives K22 even when C's change landed. Fail-closed; frequency unknown.
- **(j) (R13Δ) Branch-side relevance and superseded:** a C-text path whose End is non-textual is treated as superseded. If a branch commit made the file binary while keeping C's text inside it (a text-in-binary container), the change is not tracked. Accepted as superseded; not otherwise detectable without textconv, which is forbidden.
- **(k) (R13Δ) Chained Fk-K34** has no fixture; its reachability rests on reasoning only (§11.21).
- **(l) (R13Δ) — closed in R15 (R14-04).** AS is identical across IA7 candidates for the same X (§1.7.3; §11.34); AN differs only through Ht.
- **(o) (R15Δ, R14-02/R14-07)** Under UR-19 (b), MERGED gives up attribution outside AS (the target made the same change first and the branch absorbed it). CV7 (compiled per cell, R17Δ) and §1.9 (2) say so; it is not a state-level false MERGED.
- **(p) (R15Δ, R14-05)** Under (b), a mode-change or (iv)-only path with r ≠ e is outside AS and gives K31 instead of K32 (FX-K31o). Trace only; both AMBIGUOUS.
- **(q) (R15Δ; R16Δ corrected)** A failing git call in a computation that always runs (events, PR, content) raises C10 even when landing was skipped; a phase hook raises C13; only failures inside landing itself depend on AN (FX-C13b vs §11.40). Determinate under the read discipline: staged AN reads, one canonical order, first failure decides (R17Δ, §11.47; the Revision 16 claim of determinacy was premature — R16-03).
- **(r) (R16Δ)** CV1/CV7 texts are still O P-21; the owner may reword them, but PV-28's constraint (no unchecked anchoring claim) should bind any rewording.
- **(s) (R16Δ; R17Δ restated)** Under (b′) with Option A, anchoring never runs, even where landing rests on counts; CV7 says so. The carried check still catches a target-first identical addition absorbed by the branch (S_A > End − Bu → K33), so the practical attribution loss is smaller (§11.46).
- **(dd) (R21Δ)** UR-22's interim is stricter than Revision 20 in two histories (FX-K21d: MERGED → K21; FX-K22m-type: MERGED + CV3 → K22). Frequency unmeasured.
- **(ee) (R21Δ)** The relevance claim's scope is paths(C): a later branch rename moves C's content to a path outside paths(C) and it is not examined; CV3's "replaced" then means "moved" (consistent with L-4).
- **(bb) (R20Δ)** The relevance argument (§1.7.3) is one line and kind-independent; it assumes only that "part of C's change is present at the branch end" implies a difference of entry from C's parent. The next reviewer should attack that implication first.
- **(cc) (R20Δ)** L-29 (over-selection) and L-28 (blob limit) are fail-closed costs of unmeasured frequency; together with L-21 (Option B's K31 on squashes) they may make many real SPSM integrations AMBIGUOUS. Measuring them needs separate authorisation (RT-10).
- **(y) (R19Δ)** K21's reason `superseded-in-branch` does not claim that a mode or uncounted change of C was superseded; such a change may have survived and landed (§1.7.4 state 2).
- **(z) (R19Δ)** L-27 is a new fail-closed cost; its frequency is unmeasured. R18-01 was the third false MERGED in the relevance family (R12-01, R17-01, R18-01); the author offered the owner a structurally simpler relevance rule (a path is relevant iff the branch end still differs from C's parent there), which the owner did not adopt for this revision — the targeted fix was chosen. The next reviewer should again attack the universal survival claim first.
- **(aa) (R19Δ)** `cat-file -s` returning an exact size, and its argv being answered from a cache, are assumptions on git behaviour (UR-16-style verification).
- **(v) (R18Δ)** R17-01 shows that a normative claim ("a surviving change cannot drop out") was false for five revisions without any reviewer catching it until R17. Other universally stated claims (§11.30, §11.36) are reasoned, not proven; the next reviewer should attack them first.
- **(w) (R18Δ)** L-26 (file ↔ directory through merge or squash → K31) and FX-K20e (mode-only binary change → K20, was MERGED) are new fail-closed costs.
- **(x) (R18Δ)** The argv first-occurrence rule assumes a call's result can be reused; a harness that wants to fail a later identical call cannot express it — by design.
- **(t) (R17Δ)** CV1 clause A enumerates the change kinds in words; if the owner rewords it (P-21), PV-28 must still hold: the clause must cover exactly the relevant paths.
- **(u) (R17Δ)** The canonical read order fixes which of several simultaneous failures is reported; it is a specification choice, not a claim about which failure is more important.
- **(m) (R14Δ)** The corrected carried check adds K33 cases under Option A whenever the unit contains branch-local churn on a line C touches (a predecessor added it, C removed it). Their frequency is unmeasured, like the guard's.
- **(n) (R14Δ)** No Revision 13 review report is stored; R13-01 and R13-02 were taken from the owner's brief. A reviewer should judge them on the restatement in §9.00.
- **(h)** While writing §11.10 the author found that AMBIGUOUS fixtures on Φ-fresh also change freshness fields under a mandatory pin; the Bo definition was widened to cover it. A reviewer should check that no other field-level P-25 dependency remains.
